# Shared documentation drift

Find accumulated documentation problems across a repository, then turn verified proposals into one reviewable change. The shared operation has audit and resolve modes.

For example, an old guide links to a moved source file. The local scanner flags the link; the shared workflow reads the guide and current source before proposing the correction. A stale audit PR gets checked again before its change is reused.

| Shared in vh-adlc | Retained in the adopting repository |
| --- | --- |
| Audit and resolution procedure, finding dispositions and concise reports | Scanner implementation, roots, detector exceptions and thresholds |
| Recheck proposed edits against current source and preserve human decisions | Documentation styles, generated indexes, site and visual checks |
| Distinguish a wrong detector from a wrong interpretation | Suppressions, corrections history, schedules and publication permissions |

The scope excludes claim-by-claim docs-truth sweeps and their rolling ledger. Existing docs impact continues to handle the documentation affected by one selected code change.

## Context

### Owner summary

Move Stackweaver's scheduled documentation audit and stale audit-PR resolver into the shared vh-adlc plugin. An audit scans documentation, checks candidates against current source, obtains independent review and produces a short report. Resolution reassesses selected audit PRs and prepares a reviewed replacement. Originals close after that replacement merges and their closure is approved.

Install the plugin and native clients through their ordinary installation paths. Eight helper operations use one private external run directory containing source/configuration bindings, role captures, candidate, decisions, exact approval and partial-write records. Stackweaver supplies its scanner, twelve detector units, documentation rules, suppression/correction history, generators, site checks, publisher accounts, Monday schedule and notifications.

The worker and reviewer use separate native contexts. Recorded models, source, configuration, candidate, publisher and payload govern execution. A changed release or bound workflow requires the applicable successor checks and reviewed checklist. Bun and client version strings are diagnostic when capabilities and recorded role bindings match. Altered, missing or symlinked plugin files leave setup incomplete. A component mismatch permits static collection and reporting. Version-one execution receives its original-implementation hand-off.

Recovery uses the surviving original owner and evidence. Terminal receipt refs are retained indefinitely. Both local and scheduled reports check for a publication lease before models run. A present lease produces pending diagnostics and skips both roles. Reports check again after assessment. A failed access check leaves collection incomplete.

Administrative abandonment requires current exact owner approval and confirmation that the original executor has stopped. New hosted production plans retain the original run ID and attempt, so native stopped-job evidence can be matched to that operation. Only the present lease and its own operation's validated receipt are eligible. One atomic Git compare-and-swap push removes both, with exact object preconditions. The inspection, native stopped-executor evidence and approval are retained. Unknown accepted requests keep their unknown outcomes. Subsequent work collects current resources and gets fresh review. Unsupported lease-only deletion permits fresh assessment while an orphan receipt remains, because receipts alone contain no durable terminal status.

Stackweaver adoption requires released marketplace installation, corpus parity, native-model checks, an unattended installed synthetic trial, paired review and rollback rehearsal. Activation uses a reviewed preflight checklist, followed by a separate routing-switch result.

The earlier committed checkpoint passes 4,031 plugin tests. T143's committed recovery correction passes its focused controls, and the current corpus recheck covers 867 documents and twelve units. Fresh spec review and acceptance, final implementation review, released-install and hosted acceptance remain pending. GUI and phone checks are deferred.

### Atomic abandonment decision on 2026-11-16

The owner selected atomic administrative abandonment after qualified Claude Code Opus 5.5 diagnostic r15 established the durable-data limit. `journal.ts:502` appends outcomes locally, while `journal.ts:533` writes immutable plan-only Git metadata. Without the original runner, a receipt alone cannot distinguish an unresolved operation from a retained terminal operation. The lease is the automatic pending signal. The 2026-11-17 clarification limits the outside-helper administrative procedure to the present lease and its own operation's single validated receipt, removed in one atomic compare-and-swap push. A moved or missing approved ref rejects the entire attempt and requires reobservation plus new exact approval. Completed and other terminal receipts remain retained indefinitely. An unsupported lease-only deletion can permit a fresh green report with an orphan receipt present. This change removes the earlier lease-only-pending promise and introduces no completion record or settlement proof.

### Confirmed requirements on 2026-11-15

The owner reiterated that ordinary plugin installation and migration to shared vh-adlc with validation of all useful Stackweaver capabilities are given requirements. The earlier installation/adoption questions are resolved in favor of that approach. Use the existing shared plugin release and installation paths. Reduce machinery where the same useful outcomes and permission boundaries survive. A feature is ready for migration only after source/fixture parity, actual installed-payload checks and the declared native/scheduled/rollback observations establish those outcomes. Keep development and runtime-review provider choices distinct and preserve the selected client/model/credential configuration. The owner's selection of the recommended approach includes the previously offered acceptance conditional on a fresh clean Claude Code Opus 5.5 review. No repeated acceptance request is required for this same selected scope.

### Review lens

Architecture proportionality is a should-fix lens for this revision and its implementation review. Every retained subsystem needs a concrete audit/resolver, recovery or migration behavior and an explanation where an existing shared primitive cannot preserve that behavior. This lens favors reuse or removal when they satisfy the retained outcome. It introduces no line-count target or requirement to weaken correctness checks.

### Terms used here

| Term | Meaning | Existing criterion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Docs-drift audit | Account for the declared corpus and ground each assessed finding in current source. | AC-01, AC-02 |
| Resolve | Recheck selected proposals and record the current disposition of every selected hunk. | AC-04 |
| Prepare | Apply selected eligible corrections to an owned candidate under current permission. | AC-03, AC-05 |
| Advisory | An independently reviewed proposed PR awaiting the project's normal merge decision. | AC-05 |
| New attention | A new or materially changed unresolved decision or verification item. | AC-05 |
| Carried attention | An unresolved item whose current binding still matches eligible earlier evidence. | AC-05 |
| Carried delivery | A freshly supported correction already present in an exact open reviewed proposal. | AC-05 |
| Delivery acknowledgement | Current owner approval to preserve a proven applied delivery's later edits and stop original replay, retaining non-success. | AC-05 |
| Run directory | One private external directory holding the run's inputs, review, approved plan, journal and report. | AC-05, AC-07 |
| Migration checklist | The bound preflight record of release/install/synthetic trial/parity/final review/activation plan/rollback rehearsal, followed by a separate post-switch report. | AC-06, AC-07 |
| Component binding | The released plugin version, source commit and payload fingerprint selected by consumer policy. | AC-06, AC-07 |
| Adoption | The reviewed D activation of staged candidate C under its component binding, complete preflight checklist and exact activation diff. | AC-06 |
| Synthetic test | A hermetic fake-service or scratch-namespace check that grants no real-corpus execution. | AC-07 |
| Unattended trial | A separately authorized unattended execution through the committed trial caller, using installed code and actual model routes against the committed synthetic fixture and exact owned namespace. | AC-06, AC-07 |

#### Local history retention

Local invocation metadata uses the explicitly selected private runtime cache. `reports.max_runs` bounds the saved report pairs inspected, and `max_age_seconds` bounds eligibility. New records are indexed by invocation digest; the timestamp-addressed format remains readable. Each metadata file is byte-bounded. Completed metadata and interrupted `.pending-*` files stay in the owner-controlled cache until explicit cleanup, preserving recovery evidence. T24 and T27 carry these setup and retention instructions into the operator guides. Current implementation is in `scripts/docs-drift/local-history.ts`, `stageLocalInvocation` and `createLocalInvocationReader`, together with `report-history.ts`, `collectLocalHistory`.

#### Corpus-wide finding compatibility

The consumer can emit corpus-wide findings with no single document. The shared finding validator previously required a document path and rejected that input. T107 restores this input range before the consumer protocol correction. A corpus finding retains its scope and current source-evidence obligation. Document-bound findings retain their document quote requirement; actual edit protection and publication authority remain separately enforced.

T29 source inspection also identifies a native-provider gap. The prior role builder, process wrapper and stream decoder permitted Copilot only, while the accepted migration preserves Stackweaver's Claude scheduled scanning provider. T109 adds that missing source path before staged consumer acceptance. This does not authorize an actual provider/model trial or a scanning-provider change. The local Claude CLI reports version 2.1.325; its read-only help supplies the supported command flags. OAuth-capable configuration must remain usable because the existing scheduled workflow uses that credential path.

#### Historical glossary and context

| Term | Meaning | Existing criterion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Docs-drift audit | Account for the declared documentation corpus, report candidates and distinguish observed verification from unknowns. | AC-01, AC-02 |
| Prepare | Make only the selected eligible corrections in an owned candidate under the current permission. | AC-03, AC-05 |
| Resolve | Recheck selected proposals and feedback against current source, then classify every selected change. | AC-04 |
| Advisory | The exact independently reviewed PR handed off for the project's normal review and merge process. | AC-05 |
| New attention | A new or materially changed unresolved decision or verification item requiring owner input. | AC-05 |
| Carried attention | An unresolved item whose complete current binding matches eligible earlier evidence. | AC-05 |
| Carried delivery | A freshly supported correction already present in the exact open reviewed proposal. | AC-05 |
| Delivery acknowledgement | Explicitly approved non-success recovery disposition for a proven applied delivery whose current payload differs. | AC-05 |
| Adoption | The recorded, verified switch to the shared execution route under the consumer's scoped authority. | AC-06 |
| Trial | A separately authorized synthetic observation of the required installed and hosted execution paths. | AC-06, AC-07 |

## Scope

Deliver one shared documentation-drift workflow with audit, selected-proposal resolution and post-merge cleanup. Preserve complete collection, source-grounded assessment, independent review, concise reports, rejected-claim feedback, scheduled and interactive publisher identities, and safe recovery from interrupted writes.

Keep project scanners, detector thresholds, documentation roots, historical exceptions, feedback stores, generators, site checks, schedules, credentials and delivery permissions in each adopter. Existing change-scoped docs impact and exhaustive docs truth retain their own procedures.

Use the ordinary plugin installation and shared execution primitives. Replace this draft's private client-distribution installer, retained execution caches, adoption receipt chains, upgrade classification and historical trial/adoption glue with the setup and migration checks below. Keep the installed synthetic collector/harness needed to exercise actual installed code and model routes in the owned fixture namespace. Existing local test artifacts remain evidence of their recorded revisions.

## Design

### User workflow and local configuration

The canonical skill selects audit, resolve or post-merge cleanup from the owner's request. An ambiguous request gets one scope clarification. Audit inventories the corpus and proposes confirmed corrections. Resolve rechecks selected audit PRs against current source and combines their supported changes. Post-merge cleanup closes selected originals only after verifying the merged replacement and each original hunk's disposition.

Stackweaver keeps its existing scanner and project companion. The scanner protocol remains version 1, including raw-byte document accounting and required execution-unit accounting. The earlier separate scanner-completeness correction remains a prerequisite for adoption. The shared implementation continues using hardened Git reads, the existing strict YAML reader and docs-impact coverage matching. Current entry points are `scan.ts:187`, `snapshot.ts:248` and `settings.ts:321` and `settings.ts:453` at the implementation checkpoint recorded in Context.

The replacement committed configuration describes repository/host/default branch, documentation scope, required detector selectors/options, scanner arguments, feedback/procedure paths, generators/checks, proposal discovery, publisher accounts, report history, selected native role commands and existing credential-reference names. It uses the shared strict YAML reader. Unknown fields, incomplete setup and unreadable policy get an actionable setup result before provider execution or writes. Its schema distinguishes the replacement configuration from the unreleased staged format. Scanner protocol version 1 remains independently stable.

The normal entry is harness-agnostic. The installed skill and one shared helper serve local and scheduled callers. A scheduled wrapper supplies its repository policy, native client commands and existing secret mappings. Host capabilities and configured routes decide which actual model operations are available. A host that only supports scanning/reporting may still use those read-only operations while execution support remains visibly unclaimed.

### Installation and role execution

Use the ordinary marketplace installation, plugin revision and verified binary installer. Record the actual plugin revision, native client version, Bun version, requested model, observed model and operation target in the run. The adopter provisions its normal client and credentials. Setup checks required commands/capabilities before work. CI continues using the declared toolchain. Publication uses the selected validated run inputs without repeating an exact Bun-version equality check for every effect.

The approved component binding is distinct from the engineering docs revision passed as `--plugin-pin`. The consumer records the released plugin version, released source commit and existing component payload fingerprint. Extract/reuse `componentDigest` and the byte/inventory checks from `inspectRetainedPayload` in `bootstrap.ts` for the ordinary installed directory. Its existing private-cache ownership/0700/nlink policy is inapplicable to marketplace installation and must not be imposed on or used to chmod that installation. Resolve its actual root and verify the released regular-file inventory, executable modes and bytes without creating another copy. Installed directories and hosted plugin checkouts require exact regular-file set equality, including ignored, untracked and hidden files. Missing or extra files, symlinked entries and other nonregular entries fail. A symlink selecting the root may be canonically resolved once; entries below it cannot be symlinks. There are no installer-owned file exclusions in this design. Installer metadata is expected to live outside the selected plugin root. T130 verifies that expectation against each actual installed payload. An install that cannot satisfy the inventory gets setup-required instead of a silent exclusion. The fingerprint is SHA-256 of canonical JSON containing UTF-8-sorted `[relative_path, Git_mode, SHA256(raw_bytes)]` rows for the published plugin files. Compare the ordinary installed payload to that released inventory once per invocation. T140 removes the known inner duplicate while retaining the full installed-runtime check before client execution. T127 removes the remaining repeated entry/collector checks before final acceptance, preserving the exact byte/mode/inventory refusal and live effect gates. In both staged and adopted modes, matching the selected binding is required before any provider execution or remote mutation. New production work uses the current consumer binding. Recovery effects use the original operation component binding under the current recovery-specific permission and evidence checks described below, even when the consumer has since upgraded. They cannot authorize new business work under an old checklist. Staged synthetic trials match their declared candidate binding. Static local scan and report may run with a mismatch, record that mismatch and remain ineligible for provider execution/publication. A caller-authored report cannot supply a missing assessment or review. All other operations stop at their first provider/effect stage with setup-required when the binding differs. A source-file change under the same version fails the binding. The docs revision remains independently pinned.

Hosted execution checks out the exact published `component.source_commit` through ordinary Git/checkout tooling and invokes its plugin directory, then validates the recorded version/fingerprint. Interactive execution uses an ordinary installed directory that matches that same component binding. Both Codex and Claude support installing from an ordinary local marketplace checkout of that release. On 2026-11-15, `codex plugin marketplace add --help` returned `Add a local or Git marketplace to the configured marketplace sources`, a SOURCE accepting `a local path, owner/repo[@ref], HTTPS Git URL, or SSH Git URL`, and `--ref <REF>` with `Git ref to fetch for Git marketplace sources`. `claude plugin marketplace add --help` returned `Add a marketplace from a URL, path, or GitHub repo`. These observed help outputs establish argument support. The actual isolated release install remains a T130 check. Use isolated project/test profiles or an explicitly selected existing matching install, preserving the user's global marketplace. These are normal source/plugin installations, with normal native clients. If an interactive install contains a newer payload than the committed binding, report setup-required before effects. Never silently select latest or change the consumer binding. Rollback selects the previous published checkout/install and restores the reviewed previous consumer binding through the same path.

An installed plugin update takes effect through ordinary installation and the adopter's reviewed configuration. A pending run carries its original input and tool-version record. Changes to plugin revision, configured role provider/command/requested or observed model, source/read set, configuration, candidate, payload, authority or publisher identity require a fresh independent review and applicable current owner/procedure approval. Native client, Bun, Git and gh version strings are diagnostic only when setup capability checks and the recorded model binding still match. A resume that invokes no role compares the current configured provider/command/requested model with the original observed model and original role configuration. It performs no new provider call or model probe solely for a diagnostic version change. An actual new role invocation validates its newly observed native model/context. A failed capability/identity check remains pending. A version-only patch difference cannot by itself invalidate completed semantic review. If the original reviewed inputs cannot be established, retain the pending run and prepare a freshly reviewed plan from observed current state. Missing execution evidence is an explicit handoff.

Reuse `runOwnedClient` in `scripts/triage/owned-process.ts` for native role processes. T123 supplies watched output-file bounds, bounded in-memory capture with a drain deadline, non-throwing startup-callback cancellation, cancellation handling and output limits through `runOwnedClient` in `scripts/triage/owned-process.ts`. Preserve its defaults and existing-caller regressions. Move every current caller together before removing the duplicate executor, including scan, proposal fixed-ref fetch, native GitHub/Git mutations, preparation commands, candidate verification, role processes, history downloads and installed trial publication. Retired client version probes need no replacement caller. Native mutation responses stay in bounded memory and only their safe projections reach disk. Cancellation/timeout must clean the owned process tree and preserve failed captures. Never kill processes by executable name.

Reuse compatible execution and identity primitives from `runReviewRoute` in `scripts/loop/review-route.ts`. Its current API requires a feature document and spec/implementation review kind. Documentation assessment retains its own target and response format. Share a lower-level primitive where both real callers need it, while preserving the lifecycle review API and outcomes. Do not fabricate a feature document to run a documentation review. Existing model matchers have different alias policies, so preserve each caller's accepted/rejected identities when consolidating shared code. Lifecycle reviews retain their current PID-namespace requirement. Docs-drift gains marker-based cleanup confirmation from the shared executor. Scanner, role, mutation, fetch, verification/preparation and history results require confirmed cleanup with zero surviving owned processes. The old docs-drift implementation checked process-group exit/reaping without this confirmation. Record the actual marker or PID boundary and claim only its observed guarantees.

Worker and reviewer run in separate contexts against recorded targets. Preserve the configured worker/reviewer providers, models and credential ownership, including the consumer's existing Claude OAuth flow. A reviewer gets read-only tools. Validate its observed native model/context, successful process completion and exact source/candidate or payload target. Unsupported/mismatched identities, timeouts, errors and changed targets leave review pending. A fresh context or a configured model name alone cannot establish an independent successful review.

### Collection, assessment and protected documents

Enumerate expected documents and detector inputs from the selected committed Git tree. Compare scanner-reported inventories, raw-byte digests and per-unit outcomes with those independently derived sets. Missing/duplicate documents, skipped reads, incomplete/throwing units, changed inputs and unreadable feedback leave the assessment incomplete. Complete empty results remain distinct from missing output.

Run trusted scanner code in its owned source copy with ambient provider/publisher credentials, Git credential helpers, askpass hooks and agent sockets excluded. Preserve the caller's logical state, including HEAD, its current branch ref, staged index entries, tracked/untracked work and configuration. Use the shared snapshot's owning scope for caller refs. Exclude unrelated branches, remote-tracking refs and refs/prefetch so sibling worktrees, fetch and maintenance do not invalidate an otherwise unchanged caller. The separate owned source copy may use full ref comparison because it has no other writer. Extend the shared logical snapshot with an optional ignored-content policy, preserving its current default for lifecycle callers. Docs-drift excludes ignored build/cache content and adds a memory-only digest of effective repository-local Git configuration. Keep values out of artifacts. Use one entry/exit comparison per invocation. Stat-only index refreshes, object repacking, maintenance metadata and changes confined to ignored build/cache files do not count as semantic writes. Tracked or nonignored untracked work and local Git configuration remain protected. Actual tracked/nonignored file, staged-index or caller-current-branch changes still fail preservation checks. Keep all writes/fetches in explicit owned copies/object stores, outside the caller's Git objects. Protect foreign resources through their recorded ownership. Scanner messages, document prose and PR text provide evidence and confer no execution authority.

Read the document and relevant source before confirming each candidate. Record confirmed drift, still accurate or unknown with its reason and evidence. Preserve unknown and unassessed items. Product defects get a separate proposed repair or issue. An intended contract, historical record or deliberately incomplete compatibility matrix remains meaningful even when its implementation is incomplete.

Preserve standard feature-document normative sections, review returns, criterion proof, completed-task history and dated provenance byte-for-byte. A claimed feature document gets a handoff to its writer. Ordinary documents owned by an active feature need coordination before edits. A local-format plan permits changes only in ranges its current procedure identifies as editable. Missing or ambiguous protection policy makes preparation incomplete. Continue using the binary's actual freshness result for review staleness.

Report-only work saves its report externally. Authorized preparation changes only selected confirmed corrections in its owned candidate. Structural changes, moves, deletion, document merges, lifecycle decisions and issue creation keep their existing explicit approval requirements. Generators and site/asset checks use consumer commands.

### Resolution, feedback and reports

Collect complete native proposal identity/body and full Git diffs, including original/current heads and base objects. Recheck each hunk and record apply, modified apply, already fixed, wrong or deferred with current evidence. Preserve contradictory proposals as decisions until adjudicated. A newer PR alone cannot establish correctness.

A detector error produces a narrowly scoped suppression. An incorrect interpretation produces correction history. Preserve a real detector finding when its interpretation was wrong. Suppression retirement requires the corresponding complete execution unit and scope. Read prior rejected claims before proposing them again.

Keep a resolution record linking original proposal/hunk identities, decisions, selected edits and the verified candidate. The independent review covers the exact proposed changes and selected publication payloads. A materially changed source, candidate, target, reviewer configuration or payload needs fresh review. A disjoint default-branch advance may proceed only when the recorded read set and relevant native inputs remain valid.

Reports start with a short explanation, applied/proposed fixes, unresolved decisions and recommendations. Detailed evidence remains accessible from the report. Required missing observations and failed execution remain visible. A complete scheduled run containing only unchanged previously reported items succeeds while listing all unresolved items and owned open proposals. New attention, incomplete collection, failed review or pending publication/recovery remains non-successful.

Keep both scheduled and interactive history. Read scheduled reports through the existing authenticated GitHub reader/native artifact-download facility, selecting an exact repository, workflow, branch, event, run attempt and artifact. Keep the existing bounded ZIP reader and locked vendored dependency for history ingestion. Before materializing an entry, validate its path, unique name, regular-file kind, entry count, declared/actual per-entry size and aggregate expanded bytes. Traversal, symlinks/nonregular entries, duplicates and false sizes fail closed. The authenticated reader selects bounded archive bytes and the existing reader validates them. This retains the current installed-payload behavior without adding a system unzip dependency or a new extractor. Validate the report schema and provenance after bounded extraction. Interactive reports use an owner-private external run directory and bounded index. Repository identity, stable finding IDs, evidence changes and eligible proposal references govern carry-forward. Invalid, mismatched or incomplete required history prevents a green carry-forward claim. History grants no write authority and never transfers an interrupted run to another publisher.

### One run directory and a smaller helper interface

Store one private external run directory with a versioned manifest, source/read-set/config identity, candidate and decisions, original role results, exact approved effects, journal and report. Paths remain external to caller repositories and Git directories. Validate bounded regular files on read. Keep credentials and raw secret-bearing environments out of artifacts. Digests remain internal integrity checks over the values they actually describe.

The public helper operations are scan, report, resolve, prepare, verify, publish, resume and close. Normal execution uses the run directory, with explicit source/proposal selection at creation and explicit current approval for writes. `resume --action inspect` reads a surviving run. `resume --action restore` accepts an exact repository/operation selector and an external destination, collecting durable records into a new observation-only directory when the original directory is absent. `resume --action reconcile` and `resume --action acknowledge` invoke the existing evidence/authority-gated recovery functions. Durable recovery receipts remain retained, including those of completed operations. New version-2 plans and grants reject a `receipt-retire` effect before lease acquisition. Read-only version-1 inspection still parses the original retained format. The helper exposes no receipt-deletion action. Completed and otherwise terminal operations, including acknowledged, retired and invalidated ones, retain their immutable receipt refs indefinitely. The shared workflow offers no completed-receipt retirement procedure. Separately owner-approved administrative abandonment of unresolved operations is the exception described below. That outside-helper action supplies no terminal delivery or settlement proof and appends no terminal decision to an observation-only journal. T127 removes the obsolete general helper-retirement authority/settings rows and documents retention plus the separately scoped unresolved-operation abandonment hand-off. Separately approved outside-helper administrative abandonment follows the exact-ref compare-and-swap procedure below. Restoration alone enables none of those effects. Audit and resolution preparation remain observable stages with distinct permissions. Diagnostic output can expose an operation's pending stage without requiring callers to assemble dozens of path/hash argument pairs. `close` performs post-merge closure review and execution under its separate scope.

Private stage functions may remain when they make code and tests easier to understand. A new command or reusable abstraction requires a concrete caller and a retained user-visible need. Existing lower-level unreleased CLI commands can be removed after inventorying development consumers and replacing the staged adopter's calls and tests. Legacy Stackweaver entry points remain available until adoption.

### Publication, concurrency and recovery

Use one exact reviewed plan of ordered effects. Each effect records its native target, expected preimage, desired payload, publisher account and stable correlation marker. Persist intent before sending a mutation and record the settled outcome afterwards. Record bounded native resource IDs and applied fields needed to distinguish complete, partial and unknown delivery. Preserve the original source/plan bytes while comparing native observations separately.

Keep a repository-scoped compare-and-swap lease for cross-host writers. Actions concurrency supplements that lease. The run records the lease's exact owner and expected ref value. At run creation, write a fresh private origin nonce both into the run and into a host-local, non-shared controller record outside the run directory. Bind that record to the canonical run path and filesystem identity. Normal resume requires the matching external record as well as the run metadata. Machine ID/UUID is diagnostic and cannot prove origin. A directory copy, including a VM/container with the same reported machine ID and path, lacks the matching external origin record and is observation-only. Unknown origin is also observation-only. Supported durable restoration explicitly creates an observation-only mirror and never copies executing ownership. This is a run-directory-copy guard, with no malicious same-user forgery claim. Within that origin directory, reuse the revision-CAS attempt claim immediately before dispatch, so concurrent resumers cannot send the same effect. A copied run or another host gets observation-only inspection. This port retains the existing refusal of lease ownership by restored mirrors in `recovery.ts:328` and `:385`. It supplies no cross-host takeover operation. Unknown activity stays pending. A plain copied journal or run nonce cannot establish a new executing controller. A conflicting/unknown owner blocks writes. Normal release accounts for every effect and releases only the exact owned lease. Never infer ownership from branch or resource naming.

Before each write, recheck current permission, native repository/publisher identity, exact owned lease, source/read-set freshness, target preimage and review/payload validity. Preserve the existing live-original checks in `publish.ts:133` and `recovery.ts:138`; losing the lease prevents subsequent business dispatch. Validate immutable local inputs once into the run and reuse them during that invocation. Changes to live inputs stop the plan. Preserve whole-operation original ownership even when the current publisher map changes.

On retry, observe native state by exact resource identity and marker before replay. A uniquely matching complete effect is recorded without repeating it. An absent effect may execute once if the original reviewed plan and current authority still apply. Partial body/label/draft changes remain pending. Multiple matches, a foreign account, conflicting edits or unavailable observations remain pending. Stale review never authorizes a new write.

Owner-approved acknowledgement remains available through resume from a surviving-origin controller, normally an interactive owner operation. Acknowledgement requires a settled original mutation, direct evidence that all approved fields applied, exact original publisher/resource bindings and a fresh approval of the current changed resource and remaining cleanup. Record acknowledgement as a terminal journal decision that preserves the changed resource and prevents original business replay. Keep its non-success report and retain the lease while another effect remains unresolved. Acknowledgement uses the same plan, journal and bounded native observations. A bot-owned operation whose runner and origin are gone follows only the separate administrative-abandonment procedure below. Simplify adoption setup independently of the retained operation receipt/inspection/restoration path, preserving its original ownership and evidence requirements.

Keep durable operation plan/receipt records and the existing guarded inspection/observation-only restoration path after local runner loss. These are recovery records, distinct from the adoption/setup record chains replaced by the flat migration checklist. A restored mirror may show original intents and current native outcomes. It cannot manufacture lost native role/application proof, acquisition ownership or settlement, and it grants no business replay. Resume or exact cleanup still requires the original sufficient evidence and current scoped authority. Unknown cases retain the lease and produce an owner-action report. A stopped client, timeout or repeated absence reads cannot prove that an accepted request will never apply.

New version-2 sealed plans and operation receipts retain `approval_sha256` as the digest of exact effect grants, preserving its version-1 meaning in `sealPlan` from `journal.ts`. A distinct `policy_sha256` replaces the historical `adoption_sha256` setup-chain digest. For production context, it hashes the canonical flat preflight checklist. For trial context with collector transport, it retains the hermetic synthetic-manifest digest from `trial-context.ts:128`, as used by `trial-publication.ts:73` and `trial-recovery.ts:20`. This digest describes the fake-service fixture only.

For the unattended trial with native transport, `policy_sha256` hashes canonical JSON containing the committed trial-definition digest, candidate component binding and observed native launch record. That record binds repository numeric ID/name/host, workflow numeric ID/path, authenticated publisher numeric ID/REST login, event, head commit, run ID and attempt. The publisher is the credential-selected native viewer compared with the definition's publisher identity, independently of the account that dispatched the run. Any triggering-account observation is diagnostic only and supplies no publisher identity or grant. Expected event comes from the committed trial caller's selected trigger, `workflow_dispatch` for the owner-dispatched acceptance trial. Expected head is the exact approved dispatch ref commit, which must be the current default containing the definition when selected. Compare native run event/head/workflow/repository and publisher observations against those respective inputs. This adds the named checks to the current trial path, whose original definition and history readers do not yet validate them. The definition is separately read through `trial-definition.ts:30` and `:66`; the new native launch binding extends that path. Neither trial kind needs a future checklist or trial outcome. Context and transport disambiguate their policy digests.

Both contexts also record the explicit component binding. Recovery compares the original context-specific policy digest and component binding, not the latest consumer checklist. The explicitly selected original release reads role, publisher, scope and policy-digest bindings from the operation's original committed consumer policy. It does not parse a successor configuration through its older strict schema. Exact current owner approval of the recovery scope, current native repository/account permissions, current resource preimages and original ownership/application/settlement evidence gate recovery effects. Such exact recovery effects are exempt from the current adopted-mode/component/checklist gate for new business work. They cannot publish a new correction or replay an abandoned business plan. Ordinary business replay through resume retains all current production gates. A changed schema, missing current approval or unknown evidence supplies no standing authority.

Execution of exact surviving-origin recovery effects uses the original recorded released component through an explicitly selected ordinary matching install or released-source checkout. Current-code read-only inspection may collect a supported old record without executing that old component. Metadata never authorizes downloading or executing a component. The normal hosted job selects only its committed current release. After a consumer upgrade, that job may inspect the interrupted old operation and then hands off before recovery effects. A same-origin operator explicitly selects the original ordinary release for such effects under current exact recovery approval.

For a bot-owned hosted operation, approved recovery effects require the still-surviving original job/controller with its original origin record and credential mapping. After that runner and origin record are lost, fresh scheduled or dispatched jobs remain observation-only. Upgrading or rolling back the component binding does not restore ownership. A bot-owned lost-origin case goes to the documented separately approved administrative-abandonment procedure. The port adds no recovery-dispatch mode, cross-host takeover or credential transfer. An interactive owner remains foreign to the bot operation. There is no automatic receipt-driven checkout or new historical-release configuration list. Missing original release/approval evidence produces a handoff.

Old version-1 plans/receipts retain their original bytes and `adoption_sha256`. The new interface can inspect them, but does not reinterpret that digest or execute them as version 2. A real unfinished version-1 run requires the original implementation/setup handoff identified by T121. No private execution cache or recursive adoption chain is required for new version-2 recovery.

Hosted run directories are normally ephemeral. An interruption after lease acquisition can leave later local or scheduled jobs with only remote recovery records. Every local and scheduled `report` that reaches native assessment checks the exact configured lease before invoking either model role. A component-binding mismatch produces static source diagnostics with pending status before native assessment. A present lease produces pending diagnostics, names its exact ref/object and available receipt evidence, and skips both roles. Unreadable or foreign metadata still leaves that lease pending. If the initial lease is absent, recheck after assessment before retaining a successful report. The existing final native read-set replay still applies. Treat a ref 404 as absence only after authenticated repository metadata, default-branch ref and its commit establish Contents read access. An unavailable access proof or another failed lease lookup leaves collection incomplete. Failed diagnostic saving also leaves the result incomplete.

New version-two native production plans created through the normal helper inside GitHub Actions retain `hosted_executor: { run_id, run_attempt }` in their existing immutable `plan.json`. Repository identity and assessed source are already bound by the manifest. The normal entry uses the Actions environment only to select the run/attempt, then validates the existing authenticated native run observation against the repository, configured workflow, source, supported schedule/manual event and active execution before lease acquisition. Invalid or unavailable native attribution blocks a new hosted publication or closure. The publisher and triggering actor retain their existing separate meanings. This bounded run/attempt identity implements the owner's approved stopped-executor condition and retained native-ID evidence scope. It supplies attribution; original ownership, permission and effect checks still govern execution.

The optional member is preserved by canonical plan parsing and policy binding. `policy_sha256` keeps its production preflight-checklist meaning. The native trial keeps its existing `trial_launch` binding. Validate positive safe-integer run/attempt IDs, reject the member in other contexts and refuse a recorded identity mismatch during original execution or resume. Existing version-one and version-two records without the member remain readable with their original bytes. A hosted operation without recorded executor identity remains ineligible for this administrative procedure, pending with an explicit original-evidence hand-off. Never reconstruct that missing binding from timing, caller assertions or a later job.

Lost original ownership permits observation-only inspection. Administrative abandonment is a separately approved Git action outside automatic helper recovery. Before deletion, establish that the original executor has stopped, retaining a native execution-status observation linked to that original operation. For hosted execution, read the executor identity from the inspected retained `plan.json` and save the authenticated terminal native observation for that exact repository, run ID and attempt. An observation for another run or attempt is ineligible. For local execution, record the original owned process completion and absence of owned survivors. An active executor, unknown status or unavailable operation linkage makes the procedure ineligible. This precondition prevents further dispatch. Unknown accepted requests retain unknown outcomes and supply no settlement proof.

Only the present lease and the single receipt ref bound to that lease's operation in its validated retained plan are eligible for this procedure. Verify the repository and operation linkage through the existing inspect/restore records and plan's receipt-retain effect. Missing or unreadable plan linkage, any additional receipt, or a receipt belonging to another operation makes the proposed selection ineligible. Receipts without a present matching lease stay retained under this procedure. Completed and otherwise terminal receipts retain their immutable refs indefinitely.

Retain the existing `resume --action inspect` or `restore` output as `abandonment-inspection.json` in an owner-private external case directory. Keep the exact current owner approval in `approval.txt`, with the native stopped-executor observation beside it. The inspection identifies the operation, observed refs/objects, unknown outcomes and current resources. The owner's approval names the repository, operation and both exact ref/object pairs. This adds no abandonment helper command, status store or report schema.

Remove the lease and its operation's receipt together in one ordinary `git push --atomic`, with one exact `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<object>` per approved ref and only those two deletion refspecs. The server must support atomic push. A missing or moved selected ref or unsupported atomic operation rejects the whole attempt without deleting any ref. Reobserve changed inputs and obtain new exact approval before retrying. The authority guide documents this manual procedure and its eligibility checks. No automatic expiry or job cancellation authorizes it.

The lease is the automatic pending signal. Immutable receipts contain the approved plan, while terminal outcomes remain in the original journal, so receipts alone supply no unresolved-versus-terminal classification. After unsupported manual lease-only deletion, a fresh report performs current collection and independent assessment, reporting its own findings normally. With complete empty findings/history it returns collected/zero, retaining the orphan receipt and making no settlement, abandonment-success or replay claim. Ordinary proposal/history readers may still read receipts for their existing purpose. This limitation introduces no receipt classifier or new durable completion record.

After exact approved atomic abandonment, later jobs collect current source and resources and obtain fresh independent review and current effect approval. They never resume the abandoned plan or treat unknown effects as successful. The private case directory supplies operator evidence and is outside scheduled inputs. Preserve original payloads, reports and failed artifacts through their existing retention and never reconstruct missing proof.

Publisher selection supports the scheduled bot and interactive owner. Acquire the authenticated viewer's numeric ID, resolve it through the native REST account endpoint and retain the REST login. This handles the same bot appearing as a GraphQL slug and REST bracketed login. Compare configured/native login case-insensitively with exact numeric IDs. Every configured publisher must appear in explicit discovery authors, and a discovery prefix must cover its publication branch prefix. Keep recorded evidence unchanged through case-only later observations. A material login or numeric-ID change stops the old operation. A different authorized publisher may read eligible history and begin a freshly reviewed operation, while original-operation recovery stays bound to its original account.

### Migration, compatibility and rollout

The public shared feature remains on its owning branch. Its previous code and checked tasks are implementation history. Earlier test results keep their original commit attribution. This normative revision resets live acceptance proof and requires a fresh spec review and acceptance before implementation.

The staged Stackweaver branch still contains the old configuration and trial entry. Inventory its callers and any real retained runs before replacing unreleased formats. Preserve its separate scanner correction, detector behavior, legacy JSON/console/exits, feedback files, local procedure, existing Claude command, Monday 09:00 UTC schedule, secret mappings and notifications. Record exact shared/consumer revisions. A required old pending run gets an explicit retention/review handoff before cleanup.

Replace the historical adoption/upgrade receipt chains with one preflight checklist and a small committed mode gate. Keep `publication.mode` as staged/adopted and an approved component binding. Before every new production business write or business replay, adopted mode, the installed payload fingerprint/version match and a complete preflight checklist must pass. Exact owner-approved recovery effects follow the original-policy/current-approval rule above. A checklist is operator/reviewer evidence with validated schema and source/payload bindings. It grants no write authority and claims no resistance to malicious same-user forgery.

The binding order is explicit. First review the staged consumer candidate C with its component binding, final policy and proposed activation diff. Generate the preflight checklist from C. It records C's repository/commit, component binding, required passed checks, the normalized configuration digest and the exact approved before/after activation-file hashes. Normalization omits only `publication.mode` and `publication.checklist_path`; all other settings remain bound. Commit the checklist as an immutable consumer file and activate through a subsequent reviewed commit D. D is C's direct child and changes only the mode/checklist reference, adds that checklist and applies the recorded activation diff. Both commits remain reachable from current default; merge-commit delivery may make D an ancestor of the integration merge. The gate checks the exact C-to-D shape, the current normalized policy and current activation-file after-hashes. Intervening unrelated default commits are acceptable when those current bindings still match. Relevant integration conflicts or policy/activation changes require a new reviewed C/D pair and successor checklist, without rewriting the old reviewed commits. Subsequent unrelated product changes do not invalidate the config binding. A changed policy/component or activation-file hash needs a reviewed successor checklist under the same order. A successor or rollback may activate while an operation remains pending, provided preflight records the unresolved operation and its original release and preserves its records/lease. Activation never transfers that operation or claims its success. A hosted route without the original origin proof remains observation-only and reports the administrative-abandonment handoff, irrespective of upgrade or rollback. A rollback to a pre-version-2 release cannot resume version-2 operations and must keep them pending with their original-release handoff; restoring legacy routing supplies no cleanup authority.

The preflight checklist contains released-payload/install, real-corpus scanner parity, synthetic installed/model trial, final paired review of C and its activation diff, and rollback rehearsal results. It cannot contain the future actual routing-switch outcome. Observe actual switch/production outcomes after D in a separate delivery report that cannot supply missing preflight checks. This keeps hashes acyclic while retaining the staged production gate.

Upgrade checks use the fixed change classes below. The reviewer records the applicable rows and outcomes; mixed changes take the union. A component release that leaves the docs-drift procedure, helper dependency closure, settings/role contracts and binary interface unchanged repeats published-payload/install integrity and installed routing smoke; its review records why earlier unaffected native trials still apply at their original targets. Releases that change those dependencies use the affected change classes below. A successor checklist is a flat current record: repeat the selected rows and retain original attribution for unaffected evidence, without walking a predecessor chain. Procedure/report-only changes need relevant document/package tests and installed routing smoke. Scanner/scope/feedback changes also need scanner/protocol and corpus parity. Effect/recovery changes also need source-level fake-service interruption/concurrency checks plus installed routing smoke. Routine dependency/digest changes to bound workflow/activation files need the relevant CI/action validation and installed routing smoke; changes affecting credentials, runtime setup or permissions also need the installed synthetic workflow trial. Provider, role model/command, credential mapping or publisher identity changes need actual affected native-entry synthetic trials. Unclassified changes remain pending for scoped review.

Publication state and installation alone cannot establish operation support.

Before consumer cutover, publish the reviewed shared dependency under its configured checks, install its actual marketplace payload in isolated profiles and exercise the selected native operations. Retain the accepted unattended installed synthetic trial. It exercises the actual installed helper, native worker/reviewer and owned fixture effects, using the committed fixture definition and a namespace disjoint from real-corpus publication. The trial harness remains test-only and cannot select production effects. Ordinary production commands cannot accept a caller-provided fake transport. Real-corpus collection/parity is a separate read-only check. Actual production use after adoption retains its selected publication authority and is reported at the observed support level. Development may use an early paired review, but AC-06 requires the final paired migration review through actual Claude Code Opus 5.5, after release/install and against the exact released component binding, final staged consumer C and reviewed activation diff. T130 produces that final proof under its exact private packet approval. Any relevant later source, consumer or activation change repeats the affected review; older results keep their source attribution. The unattended synthetic grant is a committed trial definition, selected by path/digest from staged configuration and approved before dispatch. Reuse the current definition/collector validation for fixture identity, allowed branch/resource namespace and exact effects. Bind it to the component binding, entry/role configuration and committed workflow path/source. Its owner-approved definition is the scoped standing trial permission, separate from live-corpus authority. Inside the runner, verify repository/workflow and the authenticated publisher against the definition, event against the selected committed caller trigger, and head against the approved dispatch commit. Bind the actual run ID/attempt at launch. A human dispatching the trial and its bot publishing are separate identities. The grant does not need to contain its own future run ID or commit hash. Dispatch input alone cannot create it. Document this representation in the authority table.

Validate its original native model/target/context and process outcome. Automatic runtime operation reviews keep their configured provider/model/credential bindings and their own captured native identity. Keep legacy routes active until those observations and the owner's existing cutover boundary are satisfied. Final paired review includes the exact activation diff before the D switch. A disposable rollback rehearsal restores legacy routing and schedule while preserving reports and pending-operation evidence. GUI and phone checks retain their owner-approved deferrals. Other unperformed host/model surfaces remain unclaimed.

The owner confirmed ordinary installed clients and the capability-preserving checklist migration on 2026-11-15. Retain the earlier accepted durable operation inspection/restoration behavior and its limits. This direction resolves the former installation/adoption questions; a fresh clean review of this selected design satisfies the previously offered conditional acceptance. Complete collection, protected documents, independent review, both publisher identities, remembered findings and evidence-backed surviving-journal recovery remain required.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-01: An adopting repository can audit documentation and resolve stale audit proposals through one discoverable harness-agnostic workflow, retaining its scanner, detector policy and existing change-scoped docs-impact behavior.
- [ ] AC-02: Complete assessments independently match scanner document/per-unit inventories and raw-byte digests to the selected Git tree and committed selectors. Failed, skipped, partial, stale or unreadable required input remains visible and prevents a clean claim. Confirmed findings and proposed fixes carry document/source evidence.
- [ ] AC-03: Authorized preparation preserves unrelated work, intended contracts, historical evidence and local feature-review rules while producing only selected verified corrections. Caller repositories and foreign resources retain their original state.
- [ ] AC-04: Resolution retains the correct detector-versus-interpretation feedback and current disposition for every original hunk. A selected original closes only after its merged consolidation and every carried or terminal disposition are freshly verified.
- [ ] AC-05: Concise reports, independent exact-target review and declared permissions govern local and remote effects. Scheduled and interactive publisher accounts remain distinct and correctly bound. Complete unchanged carried attention can finish green. Original-operation recovery observes before replay, preserves partial/unknown outcomes as pending, supports evidence-backed owner acknowledgement and never repeats a completed write or transfers ownership. A present lease keeps both local and scheduled audits pending before model execution. Exact owner-approved administrative abandonment requires a stopped original executor and atomically removes only the present lease and its validated operation receipt. New hosted plans retain native-validated original run/attempt identity before lease acquisition, and stopped-job evidence must match it. Stale object preconditions reject all deletions. With no lease, reports assess current findings independently while retaining receipt-only evidence.
- [ ] AC-06: Stackweaver adopts the published installed workflow after scanner parity, independent paired review, installed/native operation checks and a successful unattended shared trial, with its local rules, credentials, commands, schedule and notifications preserved and a verified rollback path.
- [ ] AC-07: Recorded repository, package and portable-host checks pass for the exact released/installed payload. Native model/entry trials establish their actual supported surfaces. GUI, phone and unperformed surfaces remain explicitly deferred or unclaimed. The selected component binding gates provider execution and remote effects, with explicit read-only mismatch reporting. Simpler installation, run/recovery and migration interfaces replace the private client installer and historical adoption/runtime record machinery under the accepted tradeoffs, while retaining the test-only installed synthetic harness and durable operation recovery records.

| Criterion | Proof |
| --- | --- |
| AC-01 | Run the canonical skill against Stackweaver's bounded fixture and a second adopter with different scanner/roots/statuses/generators. Exercise audit, selected resolve and post-merge modes, ambiguous intake, missing policy/knowledge and existing docs-impact routing. Load every shipped example through the same strict reader. Verify the eight documented helper operations accept one run directory and invoke their real stage functions. Check the canonical policy references and public guides against those operations and their actual recovery/resources. |
| AC-02 | Retain protocol/scan/snapshot tests for complete empty/findings reports, exact raw-byte Git inventories, required-unit subset/zero-input selectors, skipped/throwing units, invalid encodings/paths, unknown detectors and stale source. Omitted documents and echoed fabricated digests must fail completeness. Exercise hardened Git/filter/replace/promisor controls and a scanner with canary credentials and forbidden writes. Test still-accurate historical claims and unknown/runtime-only claims alongside confirmed stale references. |
| AC-03 | Retain protection/preparation tests for declared/absent/broken roots, shared Go/TypeScript coverage parity, claimed specs, byte-identical review/proof/history records, local-format editable ranges, active writer coordination and generated files. Exercise dirty caller state and recorded owned PR stores. Report-only and failure paths must preserve logical files/index/ref state and foreign resources. A caller index stat refresh, background git maintenance and an ignored build-file change must leave collection complete. A sibling-worktree commit, user fetch or maintenance prefetch must also leave caller preservation complete. Real tracked/nonignored untracked/index/current-branch or local-config mutation must fail preservation. Lifecycle snapshot defaults remain unchanged. |
| AC-04 | Run full-diff resolution, feedback and closure tests for duplicate/conflicting/stale proposals, wrong detector versus interpretation, stale suppression retirement and binary/generated changes. Verify a complete merged consolidation closes only selected accounted-for originals, an unmerged or modified replacement leaves them open and a changed explanation needs fresh review. |
| AC-05 | Run real helper/native fake-service paths for independently reviewed audit/publication/closure and both bot/owner accounts. Test before-send failure, response loss, intent/outcome interruption, partial text/labels/draft, concurrent leases, two same-directory resumers and copied-run observation-only refusals, same-account recasing, changed ID/material login, foreign recovery and stale review. Preserve original plan/evidence bytes and count remote effects once. Exercise exact owner acknowledgement, missing/contradictory application evidence, unresolved neighboring effects and cleanup crashes. Verify green unchanged attention, new/changed attention, incomplete history and cross-entry read-only history without ownership transfer. End a hosted job after lease acquisition, discard its local directory and run the next schedule on a fresh runner. Restore bounded remote inspection context, require zero business writes and report pending ownership/application proof with the lease retained. A stopped client and repeated absence reads cannot replace settlement evidence. Test the documented outside-helper Git abandonment procedure using saved inspect/restore JSON and adjacent exact approval, requiring its non-success resource/outcome fields and no new abandonment command. Test both local and scheduled entries: a present lease skips both models and returns pending, while an initially absent lease acquired during assessment prevents a green report. Cover readable/foreign/unreadable metadata, failed lease lookup and a 404 without established Contents access, requiring incomplete status for unavailable access. Verify new hosted publication/closure plans retain native-validated run/attempt identity before lease acquisition. Reject missing/invalid selectors, native repository/workflow/source/event mismatch and unavailable run observations. Read old version-one/version-two records byte-for-byte without adding identity. Rehearse manual eligibility against the recorded identity: absent identity, another run or attempt, or unavailable terminal native status remains ineligible and pending with an explicit original-evidence hand-off. Keep native trial attribution separate. Rehearse the documented manual eligibility checks with saved native stopped-executor evidence linked to the original operation. A running or unknown executor, missing linkage, a receipt-only selection or an additional foreign/terminal receipt makes the procedure ineligible and sends no deletion. Preserve the existing live-original per-effect lease fence and test lease removal while that controller remains alive. Test one atomic `git push --atomic` with exact expected objects for the present lease and its operation receipt. A stale or missing approved ref or atomic-disabled server refuses the whole push and preserves every ref. In the fixed empty-findings/no-proposal fixture, unsupported manual lease-only deletion requires fresh independent assessment and collected/zero, with the orphan receipt unchanged, no abandoned-plan replay and no settlement or abandonment-success claim. Exact approved atomic abandonment permits only a fresh reviewed plan from current resources. Preserve every terminal receipt belonging to another operation. A copied run with identical machine ID/path but no external origin record remains observation-only. Refuse all cross-host ownership transfer and helper-driven effects from a lost-origin mirror. Exact owner approval alone cannot replace the original ownership or settlement proof. Test GraphQL/REST bot representations with one numeric ID, missing discovery authors and uncovered publication prefixes. Retain ZIP traversal, duplicate-name, nonregular-entry, false-size, entry-count and expanded-byte limit controls. Require `resume --action retire-receipt` to match any other unknown action at argument parsing, with diagnostic `Select a supported resume action.`, outcome `incomplete` and exit 2. Cover both absent and existing original run directories, preserve every existing run/journal/stage byte and require zero transport calls. Help, usage and public guides list no `retire-receipt` resume action or helper receipt-deletion command. They document indefinite terminal-receipt retention and the separately scoped unresolved-operation abandonment hand-off. Require new version-2 plans/grants containing `receipt-retire` to refuse before lease acquisition, while version-1 retained records remain inspectable with their original bytes. After completed publish, closure and acknowledgement, compare each original receipt ref/object and require unchanged receipts with zero receipt-deletion transport calls. Include acknowledged, retired and invalidated terminal outcomes in retained-ref checks. No completed-receipt cleanup route, expiry or standing-procedure grant is advertised or executed. The earlier separately exact-approved unresolved-operation administrative-abandonment rehearsal remains required. |
| AC-06 | Compare legacy scanner JSON/console/exits and real-corpus collection in the isolated consumer branch. Run final bounded private paired review through actual Claude Code Opus 5.5 after release/install at the exact component binding, final staged consumer C and activation diff, under its exact packet approval. Validate original native model/target/context and process completion; changed relevant targets require a new review. Test fresh marketplace install/upgrade and installed command routing, normal client/version preflight, an authorized successful unattended installed synthetic trial in its owned namespace, legacy freeze/inventory, the actual routing switch and disposable rollback. Preserve runtime providers/credential references and notification configuration byte-for-byte unless a separately selected change requires otherwise. |
| AC-07 | Run strict TypeScript with resolved file inclusion, Go tests/vet/lint/module checks, full plugin/registration tests, package/discovery checks and Linux/macOS CI at recorded commits. Exercise unchanged loop/triage callers when shared helpers change. Test interrupted-run handling after plugin/role/source/config/candidate/payload/authority/publisher changes, requiring fresh review. A diagnostic-only Bun/Git/gh/native-client version patch keeps unchanged review usable when capabilities and the original recorded model/config binding match. Exercise resume without a role process and assert no provider call or model probe. Test lost local application evidence and refusal of helper-driven lease abandonment without original settlement proof. For a bot-owned hosted interruption followed by runner loss and upgrade, verify that current-release inspection retains the lease and reports manual administrative abandonment. A rollback, an owner dispatch and a foreign interactive owner still cannot take over or mutate that operation. Restore version-2 inspection context after removing private caches/setup chains and upgrading the consumer component. The fresh hosted current-release job inspects and hands off. A surviving-origin controller using an explicitly selected original ordinary release performs only separately approved recovery effects under its original committed policy, exact current recovery approval, current native permissions and preimages. An upgrade adding an unknown settings field does not make the old release parse that successor schema. A rollback to staged legacy routing permits only surviving-origin exact approved recovery effects while new business writes remain blocked. A lost-origin hosted mirror stays observation-only under either schema/mode. Test upgrade and rollback with pending version-2 operations, including a pre-version-2 rollback that cannot replay them. Recover an interrupted native unattended trial against its definition/component/native-launch policy digest before any checklist exists. Exercise an owner-dispatched `workflow_dispatch` run whose configured bot publishes, compare the bot REST identity with the definition and keep the triggering owner separate. Refuse wrong publisher, event or dispatch head. Separately recover a hermetic collector trial against its synthetic-manifest digest and refuse a digest or transport swap between the two. Inspect old version-1 records byte-for-byte without silently upgrading/executing them, displaying `approval_sha256` as the effect-grants digest. Independently test version-2 grants and context-specific policy-digest mismatches. Test staged production refusal, binding mismatch allowing static scan/report while blocking provider/effects in both modes, altered installed bytes, an extra/missing file or a symlinked entry under the same version, a newer marketplace payload against the old binding, wrong component binding, incomplete/mismatched checklist, direct-child C-to-D shape, merge-commit delivery, unrelated intervening commits and the fixed upgrade rows for unrelated component releases and routine activation-workflow pins. Check that selected successor checks repeat and unaffected evidence retains its original attribution. Check that preflight needs no future switch outcome and that unapproved activation changes fail. Compare before/after module/command/config surfaces and identify every retained component by a real caller and behavior. Rebuild via the actual release path, verify published integrity, perform isolated Codex/Claude marketplace install/upgrade/discovery and authorized Copilot/Claude operations using the installed payload. Preserve explicit GUI/phone deferrals. |

## Open Questions

### Q1 Hosted production selector (R-108 F-01)

Decision needed. Define the durable selector and exact native context required for a new hosted production plan.

Recommendation. Reserve the existing `hosted` execution profile for Actions. Require its run ID/attempt and authenticated native match to `reports.workflow_id`, `reports.workflow_path` and the exact assessed source commit. A missing selector or mismatched context blocks acquisition. Other profiles are local and refuse an Actions invocation.

Owner answer pending through the host question tool. Existing custom local profile names remain supported.

### Q2 Native-trial abandonment (R-108 F-02)

Decision needed. Define whether an interrupted native trial can use the same separately approved administrative abandonment.

Recommendation. Use its existing `trial_launch` run ID/attempt for exact stopped-executor evidence and apply the procedure only within that trial's original namespace. Collector-only trials remain ineligible.

Owner answer pending through the host question tool.

### R-57
