### R-60

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery, the receipt-ref and trial-cleanup paragraphs (chunk 00290 line 13; chunk 00211 line 11); Tasks T23, "Main freshness on 2026-11-13" (chunk 01059 line 7)
EVIDENCE: Every publisher creates a retained receipt ref under `publication.receipt_ref_prefix`, "for example `refs/heads/docs-drift-receipts/<operation-id>`". That ref is an evidence-only commit that is never merged. "Retirement needs separately authorized cleanup after every linked proposal/recovery need is finished". The authority paragraph also grants "exact-owned trial PR/branch cleanup" for synthetic draft-PR branches that are never merged. The current pinned delivery convention at d8f07c9 says "Before deleting a stale branch, prove its current tip is merged" and "Do not delete the default branch, protected branches, active PR branches, or branches with unpublished or unmerged commits", and "Report any branch that cannot safely be removed" (delivery.md 00001 lines 12-24). Read literally, receipt retirement and trial-branch cleanup delete branches with unmerged commits. The integrated main drift also adds a stale-branch sweep to every maintenance run. It keeps "unmerged work" and reports each kept branch with its reason (workflows.md 00211 lines 1-24; authority.md 00001 row "Cleaning stale merged branches"). Retained `refs/heads/docs-drift-receipts/*` branches would therefore show up in every consumer sweep until they are retired. T23 says the new pin updates "delivery/updater/cleanup conventions" but reports only that "The applicable new page is read". It does not reconcile the updated cleanup rule with these two owned-ref deletions or with the new sweep output. The settled behavior is not wrong: the refs hold no authored work. The gap is only that the conflict with the pinned rule goes unrecorded.
PROPOSAL: Record in Context or T23, without changing the accepted normative sections, that exact-owned, evidence-only receipt refs and synthetic trial branches hold no authored work. Say that their separately granted retirement or cleanup is an intended exception to the delivery stale-branch rule, and that maintenance sweeps will list retained receipt branches as kept unmerged branches. If the owner would rather move receipts out of the `refs/heads/` example namespace, handle that through the normal revision and re-acceptance boundary.

### R-61


FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Review Log R-60 subsection (proposal on the delivery stale branch rule) and T23 freshness/integration notes
EVIDENCE: R-60 asked for one sentence in Context or T23. That sentence would say that evidence-only receipt refs and synthetic trial branches are an intended exception to the pinned delivery rule against stale unmerged branches, and that maintenance sweeps list them as kept unmerged branches. The R-60 Review Log row calls this "one cleanup-rule context nit". The current document does not record it. Context, the T23 status and integration notes, and the later T23 notes have no statement about the exception or the sweep listing. The only text on it is the R-60 record itself. The pinned delivery convention still requires stale branches to be deleted or recorded.
PROPOSAL: Add the sentence R-60 proposed to Context or the T23 notes. Alternatively, record in the Review Log that the nit was knowingly deferred and why.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: shared/tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/role-process.test.ts, test.each "reopened new capture rechecks native launch and actual final reply" for the removed-invocation, legacy-downgrade and invocation-cwd cases
EVIDENCE: All three new negative cases end with a bare `.toThrow()` (line 125 in the first-red log; the same call is at test 00086 line 40). readNativeRoleCapture has a distinct refusal for each fault. A schema 2 capture with no invocation fails with "modern invocation record required". A legacy capture under require_invocation fails with "modern invocation capture required". A cwd mismatch throws ArtifactChanged "docs-drift original role working directory changed". The test does not check which refusal fires, so the cases would still pass if an unrelated earlier failure triggered, such as a resealed digest or binding mismatch. The cwd fault would also pass if it lost its ArtifactChanged classification, which audit-session relies on to report scope change. role-capture.test.ts asserts exact messages for the same reader.
PROPOSAL: Give each case an expected message. Assert `.toThrow(message)` for all three and `toThrow(ArtifactChanged)` for invocation-cwd, in the style of the role-capture.test.ts table.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: shared/plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-drift/audit-session.ts, reopen helper (`expected_cwd: cwd`, where `cwd = baseline.scan.checkout`); role-process.ts lines 61 and 78; role-capture.ts line 76
EVIDENCE: role-process records `invocation.cwd: realpathSync(options.cwd)` and reopens with `expected_cwd: realpathSync(options.cwd)`. audit-session passes the raw `baseline.scan.checkout` string. role-capture compares the two strings exactly and throws ArtifactChanged. If scan.checkout is ever a non-canonical path to the same directory, every audit-session reopen with require_invocation fails as "role working directory changed". That would be a false scope-change refusal even though the capture is intact. The required scope does not show that scan.checkout is canonical. Only one retained session case covers this path, and the test fixtures pass canonical paths (`expected_cwd: f.repo`), so a mismatch would not show up.
PROPOSAL: Canonicalize in one place. Either pass `realpathSync(cwd)` in audit-session's reopen, or require that scan.checkout equal its realpath at the point where audit-session binds it.

### R-62

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Audit and preparation, the feature-dialect sentence (chunk 00181 line 9). Test Plan AC-03 (chunk 00458 line 19). Tasks T23, "Upstream loop reconciliation and component111" (chunk 00995 line 5). Open Questions ("None.").
EVIDENCE: The accepted Design says the feature dialect is added "preserving the gate's `internal/doc/covers.go:MatchCovers` semantics". It calls double-star "unsupported" and reports it as a gap. AC-03 tests the feature dialect "against Go MatchCovers shared fixtures". After merge 13b789c, upstream MatchCovers matches `**` through compileDoubleStar, and lint now accepts valid `**` covers in feature documents. t23-upstream-loop-covers-native-first-red.log shows the Go matcher returning true for `src/**`, `src/**/` and `src/p**x.ts`. matching.ts still rejects them, and the reconciliation adds gate_matched overrides so that Go parity is asserted only for the non-`**` subset. So a feature document whose valid `**` covers pass the gate is now reported by docs drift as a coverage gap. The Design text still promises the gate's semantics. The reconciliation is test-only and keeps the accepted conservative behavior, which is sound. The only record of the divergence is T23 Task prose. Open Questions says "None." and the owner summary does not mention it. review-protocol.md routes judgement findings to Open Questions rather than to silent prose.
PROPOSAL: Add an Open Question for the owner without editing the accepted normative text. Option (a): keep the conservative feature subset, and at the next re-acceptance revise the Design and AC-03 wording to say "the path.Match subset of MatchCovers; double-star patterns the gate accepts remain conservative gaps". Option (b): extend the feature dialect so valid `**` follows the gate's current semantics, giving full parity. Until the owner chooses, keep the gate_matched overrides and the current TS behavior. Mention the divergence in the Owner summary.

PROPOSAL: Change the opening to "upstream covered drift makes R61 stale pending spec re-review".

### R-63

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, standard feature-document protection paragraph (normative-revision.patch 00052 line 3, "Reuse the existing double-star compilation in the shared matcher"); Test Plan AC-03 (patch 00063 line 2); T91 (spec 00828 line 11).
EVIDENCE: Go MatchCovers checks `strings.Contains(c, "**")` before the directory-suffix and literal branches (covers.go 00001 lines 18 to 35). It sends every such pattern, including escaped forms like `src/\**` and directory forms like `src/**/`, to compileDoubleStar. That function strips leading `./`, works on runes, gives `?` the meaning `[^/]`, and makes classes that never match `/`. Its own `coverageClass` comment says so, and the doc comment says these patterns follow the docs-impact matcher. The path.Match branch behaves differently: matching.ts runs `feature` input as bytes and lets classes consume `/` (`matches = (feature || char !== "/") && ...`, 00086 line 25), and the fixtures "class consumes slash" and "negated class consumes slash" confirm this. So simply dropping the `**` rejection while keeping `dialect === "feature"` would answer `a[^x]**` against `a/b` with true, where Go returns false. The vectors the revision requires cannot catch this. covers-cases.json has no `**` pattern whose class or `?` sits next to `/` or a multibyte rune. The existing feature fixtures test slash-consuming classes only without `**`. Include a class and a negated class beside `/` in a `**` pattern, `?` against a multibyte rune in a `**` pattern, `src/\**`, and `src/**/` being routed before the directory-suffix check.

### R-64

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Test Plan AC-03, the distinguishing-vector sentence (normative-revision.patch 00060 line 2, "`**/*??` against a single multibyte rune, `**/?.md` against a multibyte-rune filename"); T91 vector list (spec 00828 line 11); T91 "R63 clarification" paragraph ("distinguishing vectors explicit").
EVIDENCE: The revision gives these two vectors to show that the `**` branch uses rune input rather than the feature dialect's byte input. Neither does that as written. In matching.ts the feature dialect is already rune-aware for `?` and classes. It works out each position's decoded rune width (00001 lines 66 to 72), and the `one` token advances by that width (00086 line 14). Only `*` advances by bytes. So `**/?.md` against a filename such as `é.md` returns true on both branches, and Go's `(?:.*/)?[^/]\.md$` also returns true. It cannot distinguish the two branches. For `**/*??` the result depends on the rune's byte length. With a 2-byte rune such as `é` (c3 a9), the byte-input branch also returns false. A zero-byte star leaves `?` (width 2) and then nothing for the second `?`. A one-byte star leaves one replacement-width `?` and again nothing for the second. That equals Go's rune answer, so this vector also fails to distinguish. Only a rune of three or more bytes separates them, such as the existing fixture's `🚀` ("byte star followed by runes", `*??` true on the byte branch) or `€`. In that case the byte branch returns true and Go returns false. As written, T91 could add both vectors, see them pass with byte input kept for `**` declarations, and leave the Design's required rune input untested. No current loop or feature vector combines `**` with a multibyte path. The other listed vectors do distinguish: `a[/]**` and `a[^x]**` against `a/b` are false in Go and true through the slash-consuming feature class, and `src/**/` against a nested file distinguishes routing order. The three task-history chunks read since then (00604, 00617, 00630: T79 evidence and T80 to T90, T78) are completed historical records and do not affect the coverage revision.
PROPOSAL: In AC-03 and T91, require `**/*??` against a single rune of at least three UTF-8 bytes, for example `🚀`, with the expected Go answer false. Either drop `**/?.md` from the distinguishing list or label it a positive control. Keep the Design text unchanged, because its rune-input requirement already matches compileDoubleStar.

Test Plan AC-06 row (chunk 00531 line 1: a recased later native observation must pass "recovery and the publication gate without rewriting retained evidence or duplicating effects"). Tasks T112 (chunk 01065 line 14: "preserving original bytes/digests and one-operation ownership"). Source context: publish.ts `verifyPublicationGate` (the manifestDigest check of `gate.review.current` against `plan.manifest_sha256`, the re-sealed plan byte equality, and the exact `identity.actor !== plan.manifest.actor`). publication-controller.ts `currentState`, which re-derives the manifest and binds the reviewer capture through `target_sha256: manifestDigest(target.manifest)`, and `resumePublication`, which requires `canonicalJSON(current.plan).equals(canonicalJSON(journal.plan))`. report-history.ts `readReviewedProposal`, which builds `native.actor` from the current PR author and compares `reviewed[key] !== native[key]` for `actor`. manifests.ts `ActionManifest` (`actor` sits inside the digested manifest).
EVIDENCE: The revision says a case-only change in a later native login passes recovery and the publication gate, and that retained manifests and digests keep the original spelling. It also says every other payload, role and ownership binding is still checked. The gate and resume paths do not only compare a retained record with a structured identity. They rebuild the current ActionManifest and sealed plan from current native facts, then require digest or byte equality with the original plan. The reviewer capture is bound to that manifest digest. The reviewed-history verifier builds its native binding from the current PR author spelling. The actor string is part of each of these digested payloads. The Design does not say which spelling a re-derived current manifest, plan or native binding carries after a recasing.
- If it carries the newly observed spelling, the "every other payload/role binding" rule refuses at `current-manifest-changed`, at the plan inequality or at the reviewer target digest. That contradicts the required pass.
- If it carries the recorded original spelling once the case-insensitive login and exact ID match, that has to be stated. Otherwise it reads as substituting a spelling for the observation, which the paragraph forbids.
- A third implementer could remove the actor from these digest comparisons or lowercase it there. That would weaken the existing operation binding.
The positive cases in AC-06 and T112 would pass under the second and third designs, so the proof cannot tell a sound rebinding from a weakened one. This ambiguity is new in this revision's resolution of R-70.
PROPOSAL: State one rule in the second paragraph and use the same wording in the Durable reviewed publication bindings section. Example wording: "When a current manifest, plan, review target or native publication binding is re-derived for an existing operation or reviewed proposal, it carries the recorded original login spelling after the observed login matches it ignoring case and the numeric ID matches exactly. Digest and byte comparisons of those payloads stay exact, and the actor is never removed from or normalized inside a digested payload." In AC-06 and T112, add these refusal checks to the recased-observation positive:
- a re-derived plan whose actor field was lowercased or omitted
- a reviewer capture bound to a manifest digest carrying the new spelling

Then keep asserting unchanged original manifest and plan digests across the gate and resume.

Tasks T112 (chunk 01065; patch 00029). Source context: manifests.ts `Bindings` (`proposals_sha256`, `delivery_sha256` sit inside the digested ActionManifest). publication-controller.ts `currentState`, which refuses when `before.worker_input_sha256 !== workerInputDigest(expected.manifest.bindings)`. `resumePublication` and the gate closure, which recollect `collectProposals` and pass the original `proposal_snapshot` and `current_proposals` to `capturePublicationInventory`. audit-publication-receipts.ts `requireInventory`, which compares `canonicalDigest(inventory.proposals)` exactly over PR rows that carry `actor`.
EVIDENCE: The new rule projects only "that operation's recorded original login" onto four named payload classes. An existing operation's digest-bound inputs also contain native PR author logins inside the proposal inventory and carried-delivery evidence. These reach the manifest through `bindings.proposals_sha256` and `bindings.delivery_sha256`, through the worker-input digest that `currentState` checks against the original plan, and through the retained `proposal_snapshot` that recovery compares with current proposals. With mapped discovery, that inventory holds PRs from both configured publishers and from legacy authors that have no recorded operation spelling. The revision leaves the following open after a case-only recasing:
- whether these inventory and delivery digests count as "re-derived current manifests" and are projected
- whether they count as "raw current observations" whose changed digest is checked under "every other source, payload ... binding" and so refuses
- which spelling applies to another publisher's or a legacy author's PR, because the projection names only the operation's own actor

One implementer would project only the manifest's top-level actor field. That implementer then refuses gate and resume when the same recased account authored an inventoried or carried proposal, because the worker-input digest changes. Another implementer would project every inventoried author that matches a recorded identity. A third would refuse only for other accounts. The AC-06 and T112 recasing positive can pass under the first or second design, depending on whether its fixture includes an open earlier proposal by the recased account. So the proof does not settle the required "carried delivery ... recovery and the publication gate" pass. This gap is new in this revision's projection rule.
PROPOSAL: In the second paragraph, and with the same wording in Durable reviewed publication bindings, state how author logins inside digest-bound proposal inventory, carried-delivery and retained proposal-snapshot inputs of an existing operation are handled after a case-only recasing. Option (a): each inventoried author is compared with that operation's original snapshot by case-insensitive login and exact numeric ID, and the re-derived input digest keeps the original snapshot spelling. Option (b): any recased inventory author changes the worker inputs, and the operation needs fresh assessment and review, with the AC-06 recovery pass limited to an unchanged inventory. In AC-06 and T112, add a resume and gate case with an open earlier proposal by the recased account and one by the other configured publisher, and state the expected outcome for each.

Tasks T112 (chunk 01067: "Run a pre-change scalar-record recasing control through gate/resume, closure and reviewed-history ... the same passing case in mapped settings"). The scalar compatibility paragraph (chunk 00275). Durable reviewed publication bindings, first paragraph (chunk 00346). Source context: audit-publication-receipts.ts excerpt (`proposal.actor !== settings.publication.actor` classifies the row as "Legacy or terminal proposal has no current shared reviewed delivery"). delivery-proof.ts `readAppliedDelivery` (`options.settings.publication.actor !== plan.manifest.actor` throws "delivery-policy-scope-changed"). recovery.ts `inspectRetainedOperation` (`plan.manifest.actor !== options.settings.publication.actor`).
EVIDENCE: This revision makes the native recasing rule apply in both modes. It lists carried delivery, receipts and recovery as checks that the common rule covers in scalar settings, and it makes legacy-author collection case-insensitive in scalar settings too. context.md says the same: case-only recasing "passes identity checks across gate, resume/recovery, closure, reviewed-history and carried delivery". The new scalar control covers only gate, resume, post-merge closure and reviewed-history. Three scalar surfaces are left out:
- Carried-delivery and receipt eligibility. In the current source, the scalar audit receipt collector compares the current PR author exactly with the configured scalar actor. A same-account proposal whose native login was recased is therefore classified as legacy, and no reviewed delivery is carried for it.
- Retained-operation inspection and delivery acknowledgement. `inspectRetainedOperation` and `readAppliedDelivery` also compare the configured scalar actor exactly.
- Scalar collection of a legacy-author row matched only by its author/prefix scope with no ID fallback. Only mapped discovery is proved for this case ("proving mapped discovery still collects that recased row"). The scalar control's "retained legacy rows" can still be collected through explicit IDs.

The sentence "Every later actor comparison ..." and the carried-delivery and recovery positive in AC-06 and T112 come after the entry-map requirements, and "Exercise the same positive and refusal controls with mapped settings" places those controls in mapped mode. An implementer could add the case-insensitive comparison only on the new per-entry code path and leave these scalar guards exact as "existing validation". That implementation would pass every listed scalar and mapped control while still dropping carried delivery for a recased scalar publisher and refusing its recovery inspection. This is the same divergence R-73 F-01 raised. The revision fixes the wording but leaves these surfaces out of the proof.
PROPOSAL: Extend the AC-06 scalar control and the T112 scalar control to cover all three surfaces. Pass carried delivery and audit receipt eligibility for the recased earlier same-account proposal, keeping the original `delivery_sha256` and inventory digests. Pass read-only `resume-effects` inspection and the delivery-proof scope check for the original operation. Show that scalar discovery collects a recased legacy-author row selected only by its declared author/prefix scope, with no explicit ID. Keep the changed numeric ID and material login refusals for each surface. Optionally, narrow context.md's claim until those cases exist.

Source context: trial-context.ts `loadTrialContext` (excerpt lines 50 to 105) requires `canonicalJSON(manifest.identity)` to equal `TRIAL_IDENTITY` exactly. It refuses "synthetic settings identity" when `settings.publication.actor !== TRIAL_IDENTITY.actor`. trial-definition.ts `readTrialDefinition` refuses "consumer identity" when `identity.actor !== settings.publication.actor` and "candidate consumer selection" when `candidate.publication.actor !== settings.publication.actor`. It then builds `nativeIdentity` with `actor: settings.publication.actor`.
EVIDENCE: The Design names three groups of configured-versus-recorded guards that become case-insensitive: publication shape/gates, retained inspection and delivery-proof scope. It also requires the collector transport to keep its "original context ... gates" unchanged, and AC-06 says to "Retain every original trial context/fixture/authority gate". The trial setup readers that feed the collector and the hosted trial have their own exact actor guards, and the spec puts them in neither group. The synthetic context gate requires the configured scalar actor to equal the fixed fixture identity byte for byte. While that gate stays exact, the configured spelling cannot differ in case from the fixture's recorded identity. So the AC-06 and T112 collector case, which requires "configured/recorded/observed login casing differing at the same fixture account ID", cannot be built while every original trial context gate is kept. trial-definition.ts also refuses a configured actor whose casing differs from the definition identity. Its result records the configured spelling as the native identity. Once that refusal is relaxed, this becomes a substitution the Design forbids ("Never substitute a configured spelling for an observed identity"). Implementers will diverge:
- One relaxes these trial-context and trial-definition comparisons as configured-login validations.
- Another keeps them as retained context gates, then either cannot build the collector case or rebuilds the fixture identity to match.
- A third relaxes the definition check but keeps the configured spelling in `nativeIdentity`.
The proof does not say which is correct. This traces to the current revision, which added the collector-transport rule and the collector absent-effect case.
PROPOSAL: In the second paragraph, say how the trial setup identity comparisons are classified. Example wording: "The synthetic trial-context and trial-definition comparisons between configured `publication.actor` and the recorded fixture or definition identity are configured-login validations under the same case-insensitive rule, with exact numeric IDs. The resulting trial identity keeps the recorded or observed native spelling, never the configured one. Fixture-ID, namespace, authority and evidence-kind gates stay exact." Use the same classification in the AC-06 sentence "Retain every original trial context/fixture/authority gate". Name trial-context.ts and trial-definition.ts in T111 or T112. Add a refusal for a trial-definition native identity that carries the configured spelling.

The row's check therefore cannot be applied as written. The spec also leaves open how the case-insensitive result relates to the protected-setting rules. `publication` is a protected setting, a candidate whose actor differs only in case still changes the protected bytes, and AC-06 requires fresh affected-entry evidence for an identity-map change. The relaxed version could also accept the change silently with no affected-entry trial. This traces to the current revision, which added the table row in response to R-75 F-01.
PROPOSAL: Give candidate-versus-current selectors their own rule outside the native/recorded row. For example: "A candidate and current configured actor selector compare ignoring case for trial and candidate selection, with no numeric-ID or spelling result. Apply the same rule to mapped `entry_actors` values. In AC-06 and T111, add a control in which a candidate actor differs from the current actor only in case. It should pass candidate consumer selection with the stated upgrade classification. Keep a refusal for a candidate login that changes beyond casing. Update context.md to use the same wording.

KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Design "Publisher identity by entry", Stackweaver paragraph (line 282: "configure `hosted` as `github-actions[bot]`", "The observed actor is the PR author for reviewed-history reads and the authenticated publisher for that operation's own gate and resume", "A login change beyond casing or a different numeric ID retains the existing changed-account refusal"), and the identity-comparison table; Context T16 paragraph ("the authenticated actor query was checked against GitHub's installation-token example and User viewer/database-ID schema"); Tasks T16 evidence (line 601: "Native actor identity now uses the fixed authenticated viewer query documented for installation tokens"); Test Plan AC-05 and AC-06 rows; Tasks T111 and T112. Source context: publish.ts `verifyPublicationGate` (`identity.actor !== plan.manifest.actor`, with identity from `transport.identity()`); report-history.ts `readReviewedProposal` (`native.actor` from the REST pull `current.actor`); collector-proof.ts excerpt line 8 (fixture PR `user: { login: identity.actor, type: "Bot" }`).
EVIDENCE: The hosted entry is a GitHub App bot. Under the Design, its identity is compared across two native sources. The authenticated publisher comes from the GraphQL viewer query that T16 selected, and PR authorship for history, carried delivery and closure comes from REST pull metadata. The common rule tolerates only casing differences and refuses any other login change. GitHub's GraphQL `Bot` actor `login` is the app slug without the `[bot]` suffix (the form the GitHub CLI renders as `app/<slug>`), while REST `user.login` carries `github-actions[bot]`. This packet has no observation of what the installation-token `viewer` query returns for `GITHUB_TOKEN`. The Design neither states how bot login forms from different endpoints are reconciled nor requires them to come from one representation. Every local fixture builds the PR author and the publisher identity from the same `identity.actor` string, so no listed AC-05 or AC-06 control can tell a correct implementation from one that refuses the real bot. If the forms differ, the configured `hosted: github-actions[bot]` fails configured-versus-native validation, and the bot's own reviewed proposals read as a material login change. That would surface only in the separately authorized paid hosted trial, after T111 to T113 are built. This traces to the accepted publisher-by-entry revision, not to the current drift.
PROPOSAL: Raise an Open Question, or state explicitly, how a bot actor's login is represented and compared when the viewer and REST forms differ. For example, compare on the authoritative numeric ID with one canonical REST login form, and never strip suffixes heuristically. Add a T111/T112 local control in which the hosted publisher's authenticated identity and its REST PR author use the two documented bot forms at the same numeric ID, with the stated expected outcome.

Retain its native returned records") and first post-cutover runtime gate (spec 00040 line 3: "paired source-candidate acceptance"); Design "Upgrades after adoption" (spec 00050 line 5: "Obtain paired source/compatibility acceptance at C"); Design "Migration and rollback" (spec 00053 line 3: "Fresh paired review binds the exact installed shared payload and consumer candidate"); Test Plan AC-06 ("Verify non-circular C review then D record-only assembly") and AC-07 ("Obtain exact-payload paired acceptance before migration"); T21 "Compatible successor progress" ("require fresh paired review and three install receipts at the new C/component"). EVIDENCE: This revision's response to R-78 F-01 puts "paired reviews" and T33 on the Claude Code development-review route. It also says that route "supplies no provider-operation result". The paired source-candidate review at C is not only a development review, though. It becomes a required `paired_review` receipt in the portable adoption attestation that the runtime gate checks. In the implemented gate, adoption.ts maps `name === "paired_review"` to the `copilot` execution entry. It requires `receipt.reviewer_model` to match `bindings.settings.execution.entries.copilot.reviewer.model` with an identity source, and requires `client_bindings` to equal that entry's reviewer client. So the gate accepts paired-review evidence only from the consumer's protected Copilot-entry reviewer role. The Design also binds paired-review "identities" and reviewer bindings into the attestation. After this revision the document gives two incompatible readings. (1) T33's Claude Code review is the C paired review. Its receipt would then fail the gate's reviewer-model and client-binding checks, unless someone changes the protected reviewer binding, which context.md says does not happen. (2) T33 is an extra development review. Then no task produces the operation-bound `paired_review` receipt that D's attestation needs, and T37 does not name a reviewer either. One implementer would run only the Claude Code T33 review and relax or bypass the receipt binding. Another would add a separate Copilot-entry paired review. A per-criterion review of AC-06 and AC-07 could rule either way. Before this revision, the requested development reviewer and the consumer's reviewer role were both Copilot Opus 5.5, so T33's wording was harmless. The divergence traces to this revision's routing paragraph and the T33 edit.
PROPOSAL: State in Context which review produces the attestation's `paired_review` receipt. If it is the consumer's protected Copilot-entry reviewer role, as the accepted Design and adoption.ts imply, say so in T33 and T37, and keep the Claude Code review in T33 as an additional development review that supplies no receipt. If the owner wants Claude Code to supply that receipt, raise an Open Question. That choice changes the protected reviewer binding and needs the normal revision and re-acceptance route.

### R-80

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Acceptance Criteria AC-07 (spec 00055 line 2: "actual bounded Copilot model operations/reviews and Claude daily-command forwarding through bound roles pass through their declared synthetic transports"). Context "Claude review follow-up and native identity acquisition", third paragraph (spec 00007 line 3: "The owner-requested review-provider revision changes Design and the AC-07 proof. Publisher behavior, Scope, Acceptance Criteria, remaining proof rows and covers retain the accepted R-77 requirements"). Test Plan AC-07 row (spec 00066 line 2: "Run bounded actual Copilot worker trials and requested Claude Code Opus 5.5 independent reviews"). Design "Capability evidence by host" (spec 00032 line 15 to 00033 line 1: "The Stackweaver candidate configures each entry's reviewer as a pinned Claude Code client"). publisher-revision.patch 00016 to 00017 (AC-07 proof changed, criterion text unchanged).
EVIDENCE: This revision moves every reviewer role to Claude Code: the proof row, the Design paragraph and T113 configure each entry's reviewer, including the adoption-paired review, as a pinned Claude Code Opus 5.5 client. Copilot stays only as the worker. The AC-07 criterion was not edited and still requires that "actual bounded Copilot model operations/reviews ... pass". Under the revised Design, no Copilot-model review takes place at all. The standard says an implementation review returns one record per criterion with its exact accepted text, and its verdict rests on executed evidence for that text. One reviewer could read "Copilot model ... reviews" literally and mark AC-07 untested or not met. Another could read it as Copilot-entry reviews now performed by Claude and mark it met. The proof row and the criterion now say different things. Context also says Acceptance Criteria are unchanged, which reads as if the criterion text was deliberately kept. This document is already in a normative revision (Design and Test Plan changed), so correcting the criterion now costs no extra re-acceptance. The inconsistency traces to this revision's AC-07 proof edit.
PROPOSAL: Revise the AC-07 criterion text to match the proof. For example: "actual bounded Copilot-entry model operations, Claude Code Opus 5.5 reviews through the bound reviewer roles and Claude daily-command forwarding pass through their declared synthetic transports". FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Design "Capability evidence by host" (spec 00032 line 15 to 00033 line 1: "The Stackweaver candidate configures each entry's reviewer as a pinned Claude Code client with the full Opus 5.5 identity", "This protected reviewer change requires the existing fresh hosted and installed-entry trial observations"). Context "Claude review follow-up and native identity acquisition", first paragraph (spec 00006 line 28: "The owner's 2026-11-14 instruction selects Claude Code Opus 5.5 for feature-development spec, scoped source, implementation and paired reviews", "The owner's request for Claude Code reviews applies to that review too. This revision configures the staged Stackweaver candidate reviewer bindings"). Context "Review routing and renewed acceptance" (spec 00006 line 24: "The accepted installed-entry acceptance tests retain their named providers"). Owner summary, fifth paragraph (spec 00002 line 18). Frontmatter status_description (spec 00001 line 3: "applies that request to development reviews and the protected adoption-paired reviewer binding"). Tasks T113 (spec 00149 line 2: "Update the staged candidate reviewer bindings to pinned Claude Code Opus 5.5 for all entries"). Open Questions (spec 00180 line 9: "None."). Earlier Context on Claude spend (spec 00006 lines 10 to 15). Source context: settings.ts `executionSettings` (separate `hosted`, `copilot` and `claude` entries, each with its own reviewer client); adoption.ts `verifyRecordedReceipts` (only `paired_review` maps to the `copilot` entry reviewer; `hosted_advisory` binds the hosted reviewer model and client).
EVIDENCE: The recorded owner instruction names development reviews and paired reviews. R-79 F-01 showed that only the `paired_review` receipt is tied to a protected binding, namely `execution.entries.copilot.reviewer`. It proposed an Open Question if the owner wanted Claude Code to supply that receipt. This revision instead infers that the request "applies to that review too". It then goes further and changes every entry's runtime reviewer, including the hosted scheduled reviewer and the Claude-entry reviewer. Neither of those produces the paired receipt or a development review. That changes production behavior: every scheduled and interactive real-corpus publication and closure review would run on Claude Code. That brings a Claude reviewer credential into the hosted job, Claude usage on every scheduled run, and fresh trials for all three entries. The document already records a Claude Code review refused by its monthly spend limit, which would make scheduled runs red under the status policy. The standard says every trade-off the document settles is an open question, including one the author would answer the same way the owner would. Open Questions says "None." The status line and Owner summary mention only the adoption-paired binding, so an owner reading them would not see that the hosted scheduled reviewer changes too. The dated "Review routing" paragraph still says installed-entry tests retain their named providers. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: Raise an Open Question. For example: A, Claude Code Opus 5.5 for all three entry reviewer roles (current text); B, Claude Code only for the Copilot-entry reviewer that produces the paired receipt, with the hosted and Claude-entry reviewers unchanged; C, keep the runtime reviewers on their prior providers and run a separate Claude Code development review that supplies no receipt. Give each option's credential, cost, spend-limit and trial consequences. Alternatively, record the owner's explicit dated decision covering every entry's runtime reviewer. Then align the status line, the Owner summary and the "Review routing" paragraph with the result.

KIND: judgement

WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Design "Publisher identity by entry", Stackweaver paragraph (spec 00021 line 3: "Provider and reviewer credentials keep their existing separate scopes"). Scope (spec 00007 line 13 to 00008 line 1: "Preserve ... credential ownership", "reusable-worker secret-name mappings", "Those changes do not authorize credential rotation"). Design entry and consumer contract, scanner environment paragraph (spec 00010 line 5: provider/reviewer/publisher secrets scoped only to their later required steps). Context "Corpus-wide finding compatibility" (spec 00004 line 19: the scheduled scanning provider is Claude through its existing OAuth credential path). Tasks T29 progress (spec 00149 line 4: "Verified client distributions and the new Copilot reviewer credential remain setup requirements"). Tasks T113 (spec 00149 line 2: "Classify these protected client/model/credential inputs through the existing affected-entry trial route"). Owner summary, fifth paragraph (spec 00002 line 18). Source context: settings.ts `executionSettings` (`secret_mappings`) and `loaderSettings` (`credential_references`).
EVIDENCE: Moving the hosted and interactive reviewer roles to a Claude Code client changes protected credential inputs. The document does not say which credential each Claude reviewer uses. The hosted worker already runs Claude with the existing OAuth credential. The Design requires provider and reviewer credentials to keep separate scopes, and secret exposure to be scoped per step. One implementer could map the hosted Claude reviewer to the scanning provider's existing Claude secret, which merges the two scopes. Another could add a new reviewer-only Claude secret mapping and credential reference, which is a new secret in the consumer's Actions settings. The same question applies to the Copilot and Claude interactive entries' `credential_references`. T29 still lists "the new Copilot reviewer credential" as a pending setup requirement. T113 names "credential inputs" without saying what they are. Secret-name mappings are protected caller fields compared in the trial and C/D bindings, so the two choices produce different protected digests and different trial setups. The Owner summary's permissions content does not mention the new credential at all. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: State the credential reference and secret mapping for each Claude Code reviewer role. Say whether the hosted reviewer gets a reviewer-only secret, distinct from the scanning provider credential, and which interactive credential references the reviewer client uses. Update T29's setup requirement and T113's done condition to match. Add one sentence on the new credential to the Owner summary.

Design "Migration and rollback" (spec 00054 line 1: "Fresh paired review binds the exact installed shared payload and consumer candidate"). Design "Legacy proposals at cutover" (spec 00030 line 9: inventory after drainage and freeze "before final C paired review; if an earlier C was reviewed before the inventory stabilized, refresh/review C again"). EVIDENCE: This revision makes T33 produce "the adoption-paired C review" with "receipt-target bindings". T33 runs before T34, which publishes the shared candidate, and before T35 and T36, which install it and run the trials. The gate's `paired_review` receipt target includes `component_pin_sha256`, and the Design requires the pin to name a verified release commit. The Design also says the paired review binds "the exact installed shared payload". At T33 neither the released pin nor an installed payload exists, so no review run at T33 can produce a receipt that passes `verifyRecordedReceipts`. The legacy inventory paragraph also requires the final C review after the freeze, which belongs to T37. T37 now also produces and binds the paired receipt. Two implementers could diverge. PROPOSAL: Reword T33 as the pre-release paired migration and parity review through the staged Claude Code reviewer binding, and say it supplies no adoption receipt. EVIDENCE: This revision restores the Design sentence and the AC-07 proof row exactly to the R-77 text (R80-followup.patch 00004 line 1 to 00005 line 2, and 00016 to 00017; normative-equality.json). Both name Copilot Opus 5.5 as the "requested" independent reviewer. The same revision's Context says the owner requested Claude Code Opus 5.5 for development reviews on 2026-11-14 and that "T111, T112, T32, T33 and T38 use that route". T32 and T38 tag AC-07. In this document, "requested Copilot Opus 5.5" is the standard phrase for owner-requested development reviews. Examples: "Actual requested Copilot Opus 5.5 reviews exact `9475dc2c...`" (spec 00177 line 5), and "The owner subsequently selected Copilot Opus 5.5 for the review" (spec 00006 line 13). ### R-86

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Collection, assessment and protected documents", second paragraph ("Preserve the caller's files, index, refs and Git objects"); Design "User workflow and local configuration", second paragraph (entry point `snapshot.ts:248`); Test Plan AC-03 ("must preserve files/index/refs/objects"); Tasks T124.
EVIDENCE: This finding comes from the declared architecture proportionality lens. T124 carries the existing caller-state mechanism forward, but the Design does not say what the check observes or how often it runs. At the target, `captureRepositoryState` (snapshot.ts:248 to 266) hashes four things: the raw index file (`fileDigest(gitPath(repo, "--git-path", "index"))` at line 258), a Git-directory inventory that excludes objects, the common directory and an object-directory inventory. runScan compares this capture before and after collection and reports "caller-changed" (scan.ts:274). Some events change those digests even though docs-drift writes nothing: a stat-only index refresh, such as the one `git status` writes, or automatic maintenance in the caller's repository. A correct run then reports incomplete. T21 already saw this kind of churn ("transient Git metadata churn ... detached maintenance launches from fixture commits"). It was fixed only by disabling maintenance in owned fixtures, and that fix cannot be applied to a consumer checkout. The archived architecture diagnostic names the same cost in its finding 3. It recommends one check per invocation, limited to HEAD, index, refs and tracked content. A shared primitive already compares at content level: `snapshot` in scripts/loop/review-route.ts:73 to 100 hashes HEAD, refs, `ls-files --stage` index entries, status and file contents rather than raw index bytes. The Design does not reuse it and does not explain why it cannot preserve the outcome. The bodies of directoryInventory and objectInventory were not supplied, so whether those inventories include timestamps is not verified here. This traces to the current revision, which rewrote the Design and kept this subsystem.
PROPOSAL: State the observable caller-preservation rule: one capture per invocation comparing HEAD, refs, index entries and tracked/untracked content, with stat-only index rewrites and maintenance metadata treated as unchanged. If that is not possible, explain why the shared review-route snapshot cannot be reused. Add an AC-03 Test Plan control in which a caller index stat refresh and a background `git maintenance run` during collection leave the run complete, next to the existing real-write refusal.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Installation and role execution", third paragraph ("Reuse the existing bounded executor in `scripts/triage/owned-process.ts:60` for native role processes ... add only those needed options") and fourth paragraph ("Reuse compatible execution and identity primitives from `scripts/loop/review-route.ts:204`"); Context "Simplification basis on 2026-11-15"; Tasks T123 ("Remove the duplicate executor after parity"); Test Plan AC-07.
EVIDENCE: The docs-drift executor has callers other than role processes, and its contract covers more than output limits and cancellation. runScan calls `executeOwnedProcess` with `watched_outputs: [output]` (scan.ts:252), which bounds the scanner's `--protocol-json` file while it is being written. The executor also offers `capture: "memory"` with bounded in-memory drains and a capture deadline (docs-drift/owned-process.ts:24, 29, 58 to 66). T80 to T85 added this so native mutation output never stores raw response bytes on disk. It also supports a `started` callback that returns false to cancel startup capture (line 80). triage `runOwnedClient` writes stdout and stderr only to files (triage/owned-process.ts:67 to 68) and has no watched-output or memory-capture option. T123 removes the duplicate executor, which forces the scanner and the native transport onto the triage executor. Yet the Design lists only "output-limit and cancellation controls" as options to add. One implementer adds those two options and moves native mutations to file capture. That loses the T80 privacy property and breaks the Design's own rule to "Keep credentials and raw secret-bearing environments out of artifacts". Another keeps the docs-drift executor for those callers and cannot satisfy T123. There is a second gap. `runReviewRoute` returns pending whenever the client did not run in a PID namespace (review-route.ts:236). Docs-drift role execution today accepts the marker boundary, and the archived architecture diagnostic capture itself records `"boundary": "marker"` with confirmed cleanup (architecture-validation.json). The Design does not say whether docs-drift reviews inherit that mandatory boundary. If they do, every review stays pending on macOS interactive hosts, which AC-07 still requires. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: Name every option the shared executor must gain: watched output files, bounded memory capture with its deadline, startup-callback cancellation, output limit and cancellation. Name the callers that move to it. Alternatively, state that the scanner and native transport keep a separate executor, and drop "remove the duplicate executor". State whether docs-drift role reviews require the PID-namespace boundary or accept confirmed marker cleanup. Add T123/AC-07 controls showing that scanner watched-output bounds still hold and that native mutation output never reaches disk after the swap.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Context "Owner summary", third paragraph ("with fresh review when changed execution inputs affect a pending run"); Design "Installation and role execution", first and second paragraphs; Q1 option A ("Client/plugin changes can require fresh review for a pending run"); Test Plan AC-07 ("requiring fresh review where specified"); Tasks T122 ("changed execution inputs ... controls").
EVIDENCE: The run records "plugin revision, native client version, Bun version, requested model, observed model and operation target", and "Resuming with changed execution inputs requires a fresh independent review". The document never says which recorded fields count as execution inputs. It drops "an exact Bun-version equality check for every effect", but does not say whether a Bun, gh, Git or client patch release between an interrupted publish and its resume is a changed input. The summary's "affect" and Q1's "can require" leave that decision to the implementer. The AC-07 proof tests fresh review "where specified", but nothing specifies it. Three implementations are possible, and all pass the same proof. One treats any recorded version difference as a change. An unattended scheduled resume after a hosted runner image update then always goes pending for review, which repeats the cost the architecture diagnostic attributes to the exact Bun check (its finding 8). Another compares only the plugin revision and the role client and model. A third compares nothing. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: List the recorded fields whose change requires fresh review on resume, for example plugin revision, role client identity and observed model. List the fields recorded only for diagnosis, for example Bun, Git and gh patch versions. Use the same list in the summary and in Q1. Make the AC-07 and T122 controls change one field of each kind and state the expected outcome.

FINDING: F-04
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", first, second, fourth and sixth paragraphs; Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", first paragraph; Context "Owner summary", third paragraph; Test Plan AC-05 ("concurrent leases"); Tasks T125.
EVIDENCE: The reviewer contract asks what happens under concurrent requests and partial failure. Two retained outcomes now have no defined path. (1) Concurrency within one run. The lease is repository-scoped and records the run as its owner, so two local resumers of the same run directory both satisfy it. The Design requires recording intent before sending and observing before replay. T44 showed that marker observation alone still allows "duplicate creation from concurrent resumers". The old design closed that gap with journal revision-CAS attempt claims made immediately before dispatch. The new Design does not say how two invocations on one run directory exclude each other, and the AC-05 proof names only "concurrent leases". (2) A lost run directory while the lease is held. The Design says "Release only the run's own lease ... Never infer ownership from branch or resource naming" and "A conflicting/unknown owner blocks writes". When loss of the local journal removes the proof of ownership, the freshly reviewed replacement plan sees an unknown owner and cannot write, and nothing can release the old lease. The old design had separately authorized exact-owned lease cleanup (T43, T46, and the authority.md "Owned lease release" row). The new text retires reconstruction but gives no replacement. Implementers will diverge between a permanent block, a new owner-approved lease release and inferred ownership. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: State the exclusion rule within one run, for example an atomic claim in the run directory before each dispatch. Add a two-resumer control to AC-05 and T125 that counts each effect once. State how a lease is released when its run directory is lost. For example: only with the owner's current approval of the exact observed lease object, after observation shows no unsettled effect, recorded as non-success. Add a matching AC-05/AC-07 control.

FINDING: F-05
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Scope, third paragraph; Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", second, third and fifth paragraphs; Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", third paragraph ("Before each write, recheck ..."); Context "Owner summary", third paragraph ("Two guarantees become more practical"); Open Questions Q1.
EVIDENCE: The current runtime refuses production effects unless the committed adoption is verified. `selectApprovedRuntime` calls `verifyRuntimeAdoption` (bootstrap.ts:465, adoption.ts:602). parseSettings requires a component pin when `publication.mode` is adopted (settings.ts:334). T64 makes the runtime "deny staged production effects". Scope and Design replace "C/D adoption receipt assembly and affected-entry upgrade classification with one recorded migration checklist", and say future upgrades receive "the checks affected by that change". The pre-write recheck list (permission, identity, freshness, preimage, review/payload) no longer mentions adoption or the staged/adopted mode. No sentence says what stops a staged configuration from publishing in production, or who decides which checks an upgrade affects. One implementer keeps a committed mode gate. Another removes it along with the receipt chain and relies on the checklist. This is also a third reduction of an accepted guarantee: machine-enforced adoption, plus fresh affected-entry trial evidence for provider, identity or protected changes. The summary and Q1 present only two reductions. Q1 also bundles two separable guarantees into one choice, so the owner cannot accept ordinary client management while keeping remote restoration, or the reverse. Q1's recommendation also does not use the template's `**Recommendation:** A.` form. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: State the runtime gate that remains for production effects, for example a committed adopted mode plus the recorded checklist reference, checked before every write. State how the checks affected by an upgrade are determined. Raise the replacement of adoption receipts and upgrade classification as its own Open Question. Split Q1 into one question on installed-client management and one on recovery after lost evidence, each in template form. Update the summary to name every guarantee that is reduced.

FINDING: F-06
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Context "Terms used here", Adoption and Trial rows; Context "Historical implementation evidence before this revision" heading and the dated sections after it, including "Owner decision and publication authority on 2026-11-15"; Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", second to fourth paragraphs; Acceptance Criteria AC-06; Test Plan AC-06; Tasks T129 and T130; Owner summary, fourth paragraph.
EVIDENCE: Criteria are held to the glossary. It still defines Trial as "A separately authorized synthetic observation of the required installed and hosted execution paths" for AC-06 and AC-07. It defines Adoption as the "recorded, verified switch to the shared execution route". Both terms come from the retired receipt design. The new Design requires "a successful unattended shared trial" and a checklist "scheduled trial". It never says whether the trial runs against the synthetic fixture namespace or against the real Stackweaver corpus with real draft publication. The remaining dated text still says "The existing configured runtime reviewer roles and credential bindings supply the actual T35 operation-review proof and T37 final adoption receipt". Meanwhile the owner routed development and paired-parity reviews to Claude Code. R-79 F-01 and R-81 F-01 settled this question only for the old tasks. FINDING: F-07
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Resolution, feedback and reports", third paragraph ("Use maintained native archive handling instead of shipping a second vendored ZIP implementation solely for history"; "Enforce configured count/age/byte bounds"); Test Plan AC-05 ("incomplete history"); Tasks T126.
EVIDENCE: Actions artifacts are ZIP archives. The document's own Sources say Bun's archive API provides "tar extraction". T10 chose a bounded vendored reader so that "A copied bundle runs without node_modules, fetch or system unzip". It tested traversing and duplicate names, nonregular entries, false sizes and expansion bounds. The revision does not say what "maintained native archive handling" means, and each obvious candidate has a gap. `gh run download` extracts to disk without an expansion bound before extraction. System `unzip` is a new host dependency that the ordinary installation does not provide. Neither enforces `max_entry_bytes` before bytes are written. T126 removes the vendored path "after replacement coverage", but the proof names only changed, foreign and incomplete report controls. No traversal, duplicate-entry or expansion-bound control remains in the Test Plan. One implementer keeps equivalent bounds; another relies on gh extraction and checks sizes afterwards. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: Name the archive mechanism and the host dependency it adds. State that entry count, per-entry and total expanded bytes, path traversal, duplicate names and nonregular entries are refused before extraction, or inside a private bounded directory. Add those malicious-archive controls to the AC-05 row and to T126 before the vendored reader is removed.

FINDING: F-08
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", seventh paragraph ("Publisher selection supports the scheduled bot and interactive owner. Compare configured/native login case-insensitively with exact numeric IDs"); Design "User workflow and local configuration", third paragraph ("publisher accounts"); Test Plan AC-05; Tasks T122 and T125.
EVIDENCE: The rewritten Design condenses the accepted identity rules into two sentences, and the new Test Plan drops two controls that earlier reviews made required. First, R-78 F-02 showed that the hosted bot appears as `github-actions[bot]` in REST and as the bare slug in GraphQL, at the same numeric ID. Context "Native authenticated publisher acquisition" records T111's fix: resolve the viewer's numeric ID through REST `user/{id}` and keep the REST login. Second, R-68 F-02 and R-69 F-01 required that mapped publisher logins appear in `pr_discovery.authors` and that a discovery prefix covers `publication.branch_prefix`. settings.ts enforces both at lines 340 to 346. T122 replaces the settings schema and T125 consolidates publication. Yet the AC-05 proof names only "both bot/owner accounts", recasing and changed ID or material login. A replacement could compare the GraphQL viewer login with REST PR authors, or drop the discovery-author relation. It would then refuse the real bot or silently miss the owner's proposals, while every listed control passes with fixtures that use one string for both forms. The lens forbids assuming these stronger invariants may be silently dropped. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: State in the publisher paragraph that the publisher account is acquired by numeric ID with the REST login kept, and that each configured publisher login must be a discovery author with a covering branch prefix. Add the dual bot representation control and the missing-discovery-author refusal to the AC-05 row and to T122/T125.

### R-87

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Review Log, the R-86 row and the sentence before it (spec 0066 lines 13 to 16; R86-corrections.patch 0015 lines 15 to 19).
EVIDENCE: The table ends with the R-85 row, then a blank line, then the paragraph "Historical returned review records are preserved verbatim ... Records for R-57 and R-60 onward appear below." The R-86 row is appended directly under that paragraph. A Markdown table needs contiguous rows below its header and delimiter. A `|` line that follows a paragraph line continues that paragraph, so R-86 is no longer a row of the Review Log table. A reader of the rendered page, or a parser that reads the table as one contiguous block, would treat R-85 as the latest spec review. The R-87 row and any acceptance row that follow would land in the same place. The standard's `review_log_columns` rule and the `spec_review_fresh`, `spec_review_clean` and `accepted` conditions all depend on reading that table. I could not run the binary, so I have not observed whether its parser tolerates the gap. This traces to the current revision's append of the R-86 row.
PROPOSAL: Move the R-86 row up so it directly follows the R-85 row inside the table, and keep the historical-records sentence after the table. Run `vh-adlc review check --review R-86` and `vh-adlc next` to confirm the binary reports R-86 as the latest spec review.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Installation and role execution", third paragraph ("Move scanner, native GitHub mutation and role-process callers together") and fourth paragraph ("Docs-drift accepts its existing confirmed marker-based process cleanup on portable hosts and records that boundary honestly. It gains no stronger confinement claim from sharing an executor"); Tasks T123 ("portable marker-cleanup support"); Test Plan AC-07.
EVIDENCE: The docs-drift executor has no marker-based cleanup and no cleanup confirmation. `executeOwnedProcess` (docs-drift/owned-process.ts:22 to 123) signals only the process group (lines 31 to 33 and 92). It sets no owner marker and returns no `confirmed`, `survivors` or `boundary` field (`OwnedProcess`, lines 3 to 6). Role evidence carries that type (manifests.ts:4 and 10). `verifyRoleIdentity` checks only exit code, reaping, timeout, cancellation and output limit (manifests.ts:247). Marker cleanup with `confirmed` exists only in triage `runOwnedClient` (triage/owned-process.ts:29 to 49 and 101 to 107). The archived `"boundary": "marker"` record (architecture-validation.json) describes the reviewer harness, not docs-drift role execution. R-86 F-02 asserted "Docs-drift role execution today accepts the marker boundary" without verifying it, and this revision adopted that wording. So "existing" is false, and the Design does not say whether a docs-drift role or scanner result must now require `confirmed` true and zero survivors. One implementer adds that requirement; another keeps today's exit/reap check, and both cite the text. The caller list is also incomplete. T11 extracted this executor so that "fixed-ref authenticated fetches retain its tested group cancellation" (proposals.ts and git-read.ts in its file list), and T12 lists it for history retrieval. Neither caller group is named among the callers that move before the duplicate is removed.
PROPOSAL: Say that docs-drift role and scanner results gain marker-based cleanup from the shared executor and must report confirmed cleanup with no survivors, or state that they keep today's group-only check. Name every current caller of the docs-drift executor, including the proposal fetch and history paths, in the Design and T123.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Collection, assessment and protected documents", second paragraph ("Preserve the caller's logical state, including HEAD, its branch/owned refs, staged index entries, tracked/untracked work and configuration. Reuse the shared logical snapshot"); Test Plan AC-03; Tasks T124.
EVIDENCE: This finding comes from the declared architecture proportionality lens. The only shared logical snapshot is `snapshot` in scripts/loop/review-route.ts:73 to 100. It returns head, head_ref, refs, index, status, ignored and contents. It reads no Git configuration, so it cannot preserve the "configuration" the Design lists. The old capture covered config only through its raw Git-metadata inventory (snapshot.ts:248 to 266), which this revision drops as non-semantic. The shared snapshot also hashes the contents of every ignored file and every ignored directory tree (review-route.ts:78 and 85 to 88). The Design lists only tracked and untracked work. In an active consumer checkout, ignored build or dependency trees change whenever the caller's own tools write to them. Hashing them would recreate the false "caller-changed" failures and the cost that the archived diagnostic's finding 3 recommends removing ("narrowed to HEAD, index, refs and tracked content"). One implementer reuses the snapshot unchanged and inherits both problems. Another adds a config check and drops ignored content. The AC-03 controls (index stat refresh, git maintenance) pass either way.
PROPOSAL: State whether ignored content is part of caller preservation, and how configuration is observed: extend the shared snapshot with a scoped option, or add a separate config digest. Add an AC-03 control in which an ignored build file changes during collection, with the expected outcome stated.

FINDING: F-04
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", second paragraph ("The lease and atomic journal claim identify the active writer instance as well as the run. Acquire a revision-CAS attempt claim immediately before dispatch. A second resumer, including a copied run on another host, cannot dispatch while that claim is active or unsettled. Reuse the existing attempt/claim safeguards"); Test Plan AC-05 ("two same-directory resumers and copied-run cross-host resumers"); Tasks T125 ("concurrent/cross-host same-run claims").
EVIDENCE: A revision-CAS attempt claim lives in the run directory's journal. A run directory copied to another host carries its own copy of that journal, so a claim written on host A is invisible on host B. The existing safeguards do not solve this case. T46 records "a random run nonce before acquisition" in the original journal, and a byte copy carries that nonce, so the copy can show it owns the lease. T44 handled mirrors only through the explicit restore path, which writes an observation-only header; a plain copy has no such header. The Design does not say how "the active writer instance" differs from the run identity once the directory is copied. It also does not say how a legitimate resumer on the original host (a new process, and so a new instance) takes over from a crashed instance while a copy cannot. Implementers would diverge: a remote per-attempt claim ref; a lease CAS handover to each new instance, which races a live original writer between its recheck and dispatch; or no detection. The AC-05 copied-run control cannot pass on the mechanism as written. This traces to the current revision's response to R-86 F-04, which asked only for same-directory exclusion.
PROPOSAL: Define the cross-host exclusion mechanism: what instance identity the remote lease records, how a resumer takes it over by compare-and-swap, and what stops a live original writer from dispatching after a takeover. Alternatively, limit the claim to same-directory resumers and make copied runs observation-only until a takeover the owner approves.

FINDING: F-05
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", sixth paragraph ("only after the original writer is confirmed stopped and no mutation remains unsettled ... Missing ownership, live-writer or request-settlement evidence keeps the lease pending"); Q2 recommendation ("a stopped original writer and settled mutations"); Owner summary, third paragraph; Test Plan AC-05 ("Test exact-owner lease abandonment after journal loss with a stopped writer and settled requests"); Tasks T125.
EVIDENCE: Lease cleanup after journal loss requires proof that no mutation remains unsettled. The attempt and settlement records that would supply that proof are in the lost journal. The document's own history says remote observation alone cannot settle a request. T48: "two absence reads beyond a client timeout cannot establish that an accepted remote request will never complete". T50: "A delayed accepted create-only request can reacquire the nonce". The Design names no other settlement evidence, such as the writer's process record, an elapsed bound or owner attestation. Read literally, the precondition can never hold after real journal loss, which brings back the permanent lease block R-86 F-04 raised. Read loosely, an implementer may accept "writer stopped plus no visible effect", which the document itself says is insufficient. Neither the Design nor the AC-05 proof says who confirms the writer has stopped, or how. A fixture can satisfy "settled requests" only by inventing evidence the scenario lacks.
PROPOSAL: State which evidence can establish settlement and a stopped writer after journal loss, for example owner attestation plus a stated time bound plus a fresh observation, and accept the T48 risk explicitly. Otherwise state that the lease stays blocked until the owner performs manual cleanup outside the helper. Raise the choice in Q2 and put that exact evidence into the AC-05 control.

FINDING: F-06
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", third paragraph ("Before every production write, adopted mode, the installed revision's pin match and a complete checklist bound to the consumer/config/plugin must pass ... It records ... reviewed consumer mapping, scheduled trial, legacy inventory/freeze, routing switch and rollback result"); Context "Terms used here", Adoption and Migration checklist rows; Q3; Test Plan AC-07; Tasks T128 and T130.
EVIDENCE: Adoption is "a reviewed configuration switch from staged to adopted mode, bound to the approved plugin pin and checklist". The checklist is "bound to the consumer/config/plugin" and must already contain the "routing switch" result. If the adopted config binds the checklist and the checklist binds the config, the digests form a cycle. The routing-switch result cannot exist before the adoption commit that performs or enables the switch. The retired design broke this cycle with the reviewed source candidate C and the record-only child D (historical R-79 F-01, R-80 F-04 and T56: "the exact direct-child record-only cutover shape"). The revision removes C/D assembly without a replacement. It also does not say where the checklist lives (committed in the consumer, external, or in the run directory), which config bytes it binds (staged candidate or adopted), or how the gate checks that the adopted config differs from the bound one only by mode and reference. Implementers will build incompatible gates, and the AC-07 "incomplete/mismatched checklist" control cannot tell them apart.
PROPOSAL: Define the checklist's storage location and binding order: for example, it binds the reviewed staged config and plugin pin, and the adoption commit changes only the mode and the checklist reference, which the gate verifies. Say when the routing-switch and rollback rows are recorded relative to the first production write.

KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
