### R-88

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", second paragraph (host stamp, same-host resume, Actions concurrency; spec 0013 line 17) and sixth paragraph (local journal loss; spec 0013 line 25 to 0014 line 1); Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", first paragraph (spec 0013 line 7); Open Questions Q2 (spec 0064 lines 28 to 37); Context "Owner summary", third paragraph (spec 0001 line 79); Test Plan AC-05 (spec 0016 line 23); Tasks T125 (spec 0017 line 12).
EVIDENCE: The Design records "its origin host stamp and canonical directory", says "Normal resume requires the same host and directory", and says "A copied run or another host gets observation-only inspection and no automatic lease takeover". After loss of local records, "The helper does not clear a lease" and hands off to "manual cleanup outside the helper". The run directory is a "private external run directory", and nothing says where the scheduled hosted entry keeps it. GitHub-hosted jobs run on a fresh runner instance, so the hosted run directory disappears when the job ends, and any later job is "another host". So any scheduled publication interrupted after lease acquisition can never be resumed. Causes include a timeout, runner loss, or a cancellation by the Actions concurrency that "supplements that lease". The lease stays held, and "A conflicting/unknown owner blocks writes" stops every later scheduled publication until the owner cleans up by hand. For the scheduled bot, which is the main production path, journal loss is the normal failure outcome rather than an exception. Q2's cost column ("Lost local proof can require owner-reviewed manual cleanup") and the summary ("evidence-backed surviving-journal recovery remain required") do not tell the owner this. The retired design handled fresh-host recovery deliberately (T16 restored mirrors, T53 "fresh-host recovery guidance"). The AC-05 proof has no control for an interrupted hosted job followed by a new scheduled run. This traces to the current revision's same-host rule and Q2 limit.
PROPOSAL: State the hosted run directory's lifetime and what happens after an interrupted hosted publication: the lease stays held, later scheduled runs report pending with the observed lease, and cleanup is manual. Put this consequence in Q2's cost column and the Owner summary. Add an AC-05/T125 control: a hosted job ends after lease acquisition, and the next scheduled run on a new runner writes nothing and reports the manual-cleanup handoff. If the owner wants hosted recovery, offer it as a Q2 option.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Installation and role execution", second and third paragraphs (component binding and ordinary plugin update; spec 0012 lines 29 and 31); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", first, third and fifth paragraphs (gate, successor checklist, upgrade table; spec 0015 lines 11, 13 and 17); Open Questions Q1 and Q3; Test Plan AC-07 (spec 0017 line 2); Tasks T122 and T130.
EVIDENCE: Before every production write, the gate requires "the installed payload fingerprint/version match". The fingerprint covers "the published plugin files". The code to reuse, computeComponentIdentity, hashes the whole `plugins/vh-adlc/` tree (bootstrap.ts:35 to 51). Ordinary installation follows the published main: `codex plugin add vh-adlc@vhco-tools` "resolve[s] the published main payload ... to `0.22.0`" (spec 0005 line 13), and the installed plugin "was refreshed to that published 0.23.0 version" (spec 0007 line 12). Upstream has since moved to 0.24.1 (spec 0002 line 3). Any upstream release, including one that changes only unrelated skills, changes the whole-plugin fingerprint. The gate then refuses production writes until the consumer lands "a reviewed successor checklist under the same order". The fixed upgrade table has no row for a component release that does not touch docs-drift, so "Unclassified changes remain pending for scoped review". The Design does not say how the scheduled wrapper or the interactive Codex/Claude entries install or select the exact bound release through "the ordinary marketplace installation". It also does not say how a rollback to the previous component version is installed, or whether a successor checklist must repeat all five preflight sections or only the selected upgrade rows. One implementer pins the hosted install to the bound release. Another installs latest and lets the gate block after every release. The AC-07 control "an altered installed source file under the same version" passes under both.
PROPOSAL: State how each entry obtains the exact bound release, or that the gate refuses and the consumer must update the binding. Add an upgrade-table row for component releases, separating releases that leave docs-drift unaffected from those that change it, and say which preflight sections a successor checklist repeats. Add an AC-07 control in which a newer marketplace release is installed while the old binding is committed. If this trade-off is the owner's call, raise it under Q1 or Q3.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", third paragraph (binding order; spec 0015 line 13) and fifth paragraph (upgrade table; spec 0015 line 17); Test Plan AC-07 ("acyclic C-to-D activation order", "unapproved activation changes fail"; spec 0017 line 2); Tasks T128 and T130.
EVIDENCE: The checklist records "the exact approved before/after activation-file hashes". After that, "The gate verifies that shape and the current normalized policy and activated files". Two runtime checks are left undefined. (1) The gate checks the activated files as they are now, and the only exemption is "Subsequent unrelated product changes". The activation diff covers the scheduled workflow. Under the pinned delivery convention, Renovate keeps action digests in workflows current ("Keep GitHub Actions digest pinning enabled", delivery.md 0002 lines 13 to 18). A routine digest bump to that workflow therefore changes an activated-file hash. No upgrade row classifies it, so production writes stop pending scoped review. (2) "D changes only the mode/checklist reference, adds that checklist and applies the recorded activation diff" does not say what D is compared against at runtime. The checklist can name C but cannot name D. Intervening default-branch commits, or delivery by merge commit (delivery.md 0001 lines 78 to 79), make diff(C, D) include unrelated changes unless D must be C's direct child. The retired design required "the exact direct-child record-only cutover shape" (T56). One implementer requires that D is C's direct child reachable from the current default and compares whole current files. Another checks only the normalized digest and checklist presence. Both pass the listed AC-07 controls.
PROPOSAL: State whether the runtime gate compares current activation files with the after-hashes. If it does, add an upgrade row for routine dependency and pin updates to those files. State the exact D shape the gate checks (for example, D is a direct child of C, and both are reachable from the current default) and how merge-commit delivery and intervening commits are handled. Add matching AC-07 controls.

FINDING: F-04
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Acceptance Criteria AC-07 (spec 0016 line 13); Scope, third paragraph (spec 0011 line 11); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", sixth paragraph (spec 0015 line 21); Test Plan AC-06 and AC-07.
EVIDENCE: AC-07 says "Simpler installation, run/recovery and migration interfaces replace the private installer, receipt-chain and synthetic runtime machinery under the accepted tradeoffs". This revision's correction for R-87 F-09 changed Scope to "Keep the installed synthetic collector/harness needed to exercise actual installed code and model routes" (R87-corrections.patch 0002 lines 11 to 12). Design now says "Retain the accepted unattended installed synthetic trial ... The trial harness remains test-only". The criterion text was not updated. An implementation review must judge the exact accepted criterion text. One reviewer reads the retained installed synthetic harness as unreplaced "synthetic runtime machinery" and records AC-07 not met. Another reads the phrase as the test-only adoption gate and records it met. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: Revise AC-07 to match Scope, for example "replace the private installer, receipt-chain and retained runtime/adoption machinery, while the test-only installed synthetic harness stays outside production selection".

FINDING: F-05
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Collection, assessment and protected documents", second paragraph (spec 0013 line 13); Test Plan AC-03 (spec 0016 line 21); Tasks T124 (spec 0017 line 11).
EVIDENCE: The Design protects "HEAD, its branch/owned refs" and says "Actual file/index/ref changes still fail preservation checks", but it does not say which refs are compared. The shared snapshot offers two scopes. `full` digests every ref, and `owning` checks only HEAD and its branch because "other sessions move the shared refs" (review-route.ts:93 to 95). The current docs-drift capture digests every ref (snapshot.ts:260). With every ref compared, a commit in a sibling worktree (delivery.md 0001 lines 80 to 81 allows parallel worktrees), a user `git fetch`, or scheduled `git maintenance` prefetch writing `refs/prefetch/` changes the digest. The run then reports caller-changed, which contradicts the AC-03 requirement that "background git maintenance ... must leave collection complete". With the owning scope, a write to any other caller ref goes undetected, but the AC-03 control "Real ... ref ... mutation must fail preservation" names no ref. Both implementations pass the listed controls.
PROPOSAL: State which caller refs preservation compares, for example HEAD and its current branch only, with remote-tracking and `refs/prefetch/` refs excluded. Add AC-03/T124 controls: a sibling-worktree commit and a maintenance prefetch during collection leave collection complete, and a write to the caller's current branch fails preservation.

FINDING: F-06
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", second paragraph (spec 0013 line 17); Test Plan AC-05 ("copied-run observation-only refusals"; spec 0016 line 23); Tasks T125 (spec 0017 line 12).
EVIDENCE: Copy detection depends on "the platform machine ID/UUID" together with the canonical directory. Machine IDs are not unique across cloned VM or container images that bake in one value, and dev containers built from one image usually share their paths. On such a host, a byte copy of the run directory matches both checks and is treated as the origin. The copy also carries the original journal's run nonce, which is what proves lease ownership (T46), and attempt claims are revision-CAS writes in the local journal copy (attempts.ts:89 to 109). The original and the copy can therefore both claim and dispatch the next effect. That breaks AC-05's "never repeats a completed write" and the Design's rule that a copy is observation-only. The Design handles an unavailable machine ID but not a duplicated one, and no AC-05 control covers it. This traces to the current revision's response to R-87 F-04.
PROPOSAL: Bind the origin claim to something a directory copy does not carry, for example a host-local record outside the run directory created at run start, or state the accepted risk explicitly. Add an AC-05/T125 control in which a copy with the same reported machine ID and path stays observation-only.

This revision changed the Design and the Terms row for R-87 F-06 but left "routing switch" in the summary's checklist list. Nothing an implementer builds changes.
PROPOSAL: Change the summary to "a recorded preflight checklist of exact release/install, scanner parity, final Claude Code Opus 5.5 paired review, actual model/entry tests, a successful unattended installed synthetic trial and rollback rehearsal, with the routing switch reported separately after activation".

### R-89

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", sixth paragraph (line 505, "Simplify adoption setup independently of the retained operation receipt/inspection/restoration path") and seventh paragraph (line 507, "Keep durable operation plan/receipt records and the existing guarded inspection/observation-only restoration path"); Scope, third paragraph (line 433, "Replace this draft's private client-distribution installer, retained execution caches, adoption receipt chains"); Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", second paragraph (line 491, the eight operations); Acceptance Criteria AC-07 (line 544); Test Plan AC-01 and AC-07 (lines 550 and 556); Tasks T125 and T127 (lines 566 and 568).
EVIDENCE: The Design keeps "the existing guarded inspection/observation-only restoration path". Scope replaces the "retained execution caches, adoption receipt chains" that the path depends on in the inspected source. recovery-controller.ts:58 to 59 calls selectRecoveryRuntime with `adoption_sha256: plan.adoption_sha256`. In bootstrap.ts, selectRecoveryRuntime calls verifyRuntimeAdoption and then retainedRuntimeEntry, which loads inspectRetainedComponent and inspectRetainedClient from the private cache. recovery-controller.ts:60 to 61 refuses execution unless the running file is the recovery controller inside that retained component directory ("Original retained recovery controller required"). Every sealed plan binds `adoption_sha256` (journal.ts:18 and 82). journal.ts:147 fails with "current policy unavailable or changed" when the current policy's adoption digest differs. The document leaves four things unsaid. It does not say what replaces the adoption digest in sealed plans and receipts. It does not say whether restoration or exact cleanup runs the original component source commit or the currently bound one after an upgrade. It does not say how existing old-format records are read. It does not place restoration, reconciliation or receipt retirement among the eight operations, and a fresh hosted runner has no run directory even though "Each uses the run directory". T125 names publish, recovery, journal, delivery-ack and transport but not recovery-controller.ts or bootstrap.ts, while T127 removes "adoption-chain" glue. One implementer keeps a reduced adoption digest and a retained component for recovery. Another rebinds recovery to the hosted checkout of the current binding. Both pass the listed AC-05 and AC-07 controls. This traces to the R-88 correction that withdrew the removal of these records.
PROPOSAL: State what replaces `adoption_sha256` in plans, receipts and the recovery policy check, which component source restoration and cleanup run, how pre-change records are read or handed off, and which of the eight operations creates the observation-only mirror and performs reconciliation. Name recovery-controller.ts, bootstrap.ts and journal.ts in T125, with a control that runs restoration after the adoption chain and retained caches are removed and after a component upgrade.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", seventh and eighth paragraphs (lines 507 and 509); plugins/vh-adlc/references/authority.md, docs-drift rows "Owned lease release" and "Receipt retirement"; Test Plan AC-05 and AC-07 (lines 554 and 556); Tasks T125 (line 566).
EVIDENCE: Line 509 says the next scheduled job "performs no business write while ownership/application/request evidence is unresolved" and that "Separately authorized manual cleanup may still be necessary". The R-88 correction deleted the paragraph that defined this cleanup: "Manual cleanup requires its own exact owner authorization and records non-success abandonment, without inventing application proof. A newly reviewed business plan accounts for observed current resources after that separate cleanup" (R88-corrections.patch 0004 line 7). Nothing replaces it. In authority.md, "Owned lease release" requires "original ownership and exact object comparison", and "Receipt retirement" applies only "after linked proposal/recovery needs finish". A hosted job that lost its run directory and origin record cannot show either. The AC-07 proof still requires "refusal of helper-driven lease abandonment without original settlement proof". The document does not say whether cleanup is a helper operation or an outside Git action, which authority row grants it, or what record marks the operation abandoned. It also does not say what the next scheduled run does when the lease is gone but the unresolved receipts remain: start a fresh plan, or keep reporting pending. On the scheduled bot path, a timeout or a cancelled job produces this state routinely, so the weekly schedule can stay non-successful indefinitely. AC-05 has no control for the run after cleanup.
PROPOSAL: Restore a definition of post-loss cleanup: who performs it, under which authority row, the non-success abandonment record it leaves, and how a later scheduled run treats the old operation's receipts and observed resources. Add an AC-05/T125 control in which cleanup follows a lost hosted run and the next schedule proceeds, or stays pending, as specified.

KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix

Line 453: "If an interactive install contains a newer payload than the committed binding, report setup-required before effects". Line 520: "Before every production write, adopted mode, the installed payload fingerprint/version match and a complete preflight checklist must pass". ### R-90

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans and operation receipts bind `approval_sha256`"; Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", second paragraph (`resume --action inspect`); Test Plan AC-07 ("selecting the original ordinary released component and flat approval", "Inspect old version-1 records byte-for-byte"); Tasks T125 ("Replace setup-chain binding with the version-2 flat approval/component binding").
EVIDENCE: The Design says version-2 plans and receipts "bind `approval_sha256`, the canonical digest of the flat preflight record, and the explicit component binding. It replaces the historical `adoption_sha256` setup-chain digest." The current sealed plan already has a field called `approval_sha256` with a different meaning. journal.ts:17 declares `approval_sha256: string` next to `adoption_sha256`. journal.ts:130 sets it to `sha256(canonicalJSON(options.grants))`, which is the digest of the effect grants. journal.ts:82 and :87 require and hash both fields. So version-1 records hold an `approval_sha256` that means the grants digest. The new text gives that same name to the checklist digest, and it does not say whether the grants digest survives in version 2, or under what name. The same paragraph requires that the interface "does not reinterpret" version-1 digests. An inspector that reads `approval_sha256` by field name would show a version-1 grants digest as a checklist digest. One implementer renames `adoption_sha256` to `approval_sha256` and drops or renames the grants digest. Another adds a new checklist field and keeps the grants digest. Both pass the AC-07 controls as written. The Design also says "Digests remain internal integrity checks over the values they actually describe." This traces to the R-89 F-01 correction.
PROPOSAL: Give the checklist digest a name that no version-1 field uses, such as `checklist_sha256`, or say that version 2 renames the grants digest. State that version 2 still binds the effect-grant digest. Add an AC-07 control in which a version-1 plan's `approval_sha256` is shown as the grants digest, not as a checklist digest.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans"; Design "Installation and role execution", second paragraph ("Staged synthetic trials match their declared candidate binding"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", fifth paragraph ("The preflight checklist contains released-payload/install, real-corpus scanner parity, synthetic installed/model trial ... It cannot contain the future actual routing-switch outcome") and the unattended-trial paragraph; Test Plan AC-05 and AC-07; Tasks T125 and T130.
EVIDENCE: The Design says every new version-2 sealed plan and receipt binds "the canonical digest of the flat preflight record". The preflight record includes the result of the "synthetic installed/model trial". The unattended trial publishes owned fixture effects through the same plan, lease and receipt path. So its plans are sealed before any preflight record exists, and the record later holds the trial's own outcome. A trial plan cannot bind the digest of that record. The current code binds trial plans to something else: trial-publication.ts:73 and :77 and trial-recovery.ts:20 and :31 use `adoption_sha256: context.context_sha256` with `context: "trial"`. The revision does not say what a version-2 trial plan or receipt binds in place of the checklist digest. It also does not say what trial recovery compares against. The Design avoids this kind of cycle for the routing switch ("This keeps hashes acyclic") but not for the trial. One implementer binds trial plans to the trial definition digest and the candidate binding. Another leaves the checklist field null or empty for trials. Both pass the listed trial and interruption controls. This traces to the R-89 F-01 correction.
PROPOSAL: State that version-2 trial-context plans and receipts bind the approved trial definition digest and the candidate component binding in place of the checklist digest, and that trial recovery compares those. Add an AC-07 control for an interrupted synthetic trial that is recovered under the version-2 trial binding before any checklist exists.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans" ("Restoration and exact cleanup select the original recorded released component through an ordinary matching install or released-source checkout, even after the consumer upgrades. Metadata never authorizes downloading or executing a component") and the following hosted-run paragraph; Design "Installation and role execution", second paragraph ("In both staged and adopted modes, matching the selected binding is required before any provider execution or remote mutation") and third paragraph ("Hosted execution checks out the exact published `component.source_commit`", "Rollback selects the previous published checkout/install"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", third paragraph ("Before every production write ... the installed payload fingerprint/version match") and the upgrade-table paragraph; Test Plan AC-05 and AC-07; Tasks T125.
EVIDENCE: Two rules conflict for recovery after an upgrade. `resume --action reconcile`, `acknowledge` and `retire-receipt` are remote mutations. The binding rule says every remote mutation needs the installed payload to match "the selected binding", which is the current consumer binding. The recovery rule says exact cleanup runs "the original recorded released component" even after the consumer upgrades. After an upgrade those components differ, so one of the two rules has to give way, and the document does not say which. The hosted path also has no defined source for the original component. The scheduled job checks out the `component.source_commit` of the current binding. The only record of the original commit is receipt metadata, and "Metadata never authorizes downloading or executing a component." The AC-05 control for a lost hosted run runs the next schedule at the same version only. The AC-07 control "selecting the original ordinary released component" does not name the host or say which input selects that release. The upgrade-table paragraph also does not say whether a successor checklist may activate while version-2 operations are still pending. Rollback "selects the previous published checkout/install". If that release predates version 2, it cannot read version-2 records. One implementer has the scheduled workflow check out the receipt's source commit. Another hands off every post-upgrade recovery. A third blocks upgrades while receipts are pending. All three pass the listed controls.
PROPOSAL: Say whether recovery effects are checked against the original binding or the current one. Name the committed input that lets a hosted job check out an original release, or state that hosted post-upgrade recovery only inspects and then hands off. Say whether a successor checklist or a rollback may activate while version-2 operations are pending. Add AC-05 and AC-07 controls for a hosted interruption followed by a consumer upgrade, and for a rollback, before recovery.

FINDING: F-04
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", hosted-run paragraph ("It is an administrative Git action outside automatic helper recovery ... Before removal, retain a private non-success abandonment report"); Test Plan AC-05 ("Test exact outside-helper administrative abandonment with its non-success report"); Tasks T125 ("test post-loss administrative cleanup and the next schedule"); plugins/vh-adlc/references/authority.md, docs-drift table, which has no row for this action yet (T127).
EVIDENCE: The Design requires a private abandonment report "with the operation/observed refs, unknown outcomes, approval and observed current resources" before the compare-and-swap removal. It does not say what produces that report, what format or location it uses, or whether any later run reads it. "Outside automatic helper recovery" can mean a helper command that the owner runs by hand or a plain Git procedure described in the authority guide. The AC-05 row tests the abandonment "with its non-success report", but nothing names the component under test. The declared proportionality lens favours reuse: `resume --action inspect` and `restore` already gather the operation refs and observed resources that the report needs. One implementer adds a new abandonment subcommand and report schema. Another documents a manual procedure that saves the inspect output. The AC-05 row passes both.
PROPOSAL: State that the abandonment report is the saved `resume --action inspect` (or `restore`) output plus the recorded owner approval, written to a named private location, and that the compare-and-swap removal is a documented Git procedure with no new helper command. Make the AC-05 row check that report's contents and the next schedule's behaviour.

### R-91

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans" ("For trial context, it retains the existing trial-context digest semantics from `trial-publication.ts:73` and `trial-recovery.ts:20`, binding the approved trial definition, candidate component binding and observed native launch context"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", unattended-trial paragraph ("Reuse the current definition/collector validation", "bind the actual run ID/attempt at launch"); Context "Terms used here", rows "Synthetic test" and "Unattended trial"; Test Plan AC-07 ("Recover an interrupted synthetic trial using its original trial-context digest before any checklist exists"); Tasks T125 ("preserve pre-checklist trial recovery").
EVIDENCE: The cited code does not have the semantics the sentence gives it. trial-publication.ts:73 and :77 and trial-recovery.ts:20 and :31 use `context.context_sha256`. trial-context.ts:128 sets that value to `sha256(bytes)` of `.vh-adlc/docs-drift-trial.json` (trial-context.ts:11, :73). That manifest is a hermetic fake-service fixture: kind `docs-drift-synthetic-context` (trial-context.ts:75 to :77), the fixed identity `fixture/docs` with actor `fixture-bot` (trial-context.ts:12 and :80), hostname `docs-drift.invalid` (trial-context.ts:85), local bare origin and tracker repositories (trial-context.ts:64 to :69), and a grant that must be `context: trial`, `transport: collector`, `scope: synthetic-only` (trial-context.ts:111). It hashes no committed `docs-drift-trial` definition. That definition is read separately through `settings.trial` in trial-definition.ts:30 and :66, with a real owned namespace and lease ref at trial-definition.ts:165. The manifest also hashes no native repository, workflow, actor, run ID or attempt. Every current `context: "trial"` path requires the collector transport: trial-recovery.ts:20, collector-refs.ts:23 and collector-proof.ts:136. So "existing trial-context digest semantics" belongs to the Terms row "Synthetic test", not to the "Unattended trial", which publishes native effects in an owned namespace. One implementer reuses the hermetic `context_sha256` for the native unattended trial. Another builds a new digest over the definition digest, the candidate binding and the launch record. The AC-07 trial-recovery control passes for both, because it does not say which trial kind it interrupts. This traces to the R-90 F-02 correction.
PROPOSAL: Keep the collector-context digest for hermetic fake-service tests only. Define the version-2 unattended-trial `policy_sha256` as the canonical digest of the committed trial definition digest, the candidate component binding and the native launch record (repository, workflow, actor, event, head, run ID and attempt), and correct the citation. Split the AC-07 control into an interrupted native unattended trial and a hermetic collector trial, each recovered against its own context digest.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans" ("Recovery compares the original context-specific policy digest and component binding, not the latest consumer checklist. Source and authority checks still use current policy", "The operator explicitly selects the original ordinary release for such effects under current exact recovery approval"); Design "Installation and role execution", second paragraph ("Recovery effects use the original operation component binding under the current recovery-specific permission and evidence checks"); Design "User workflow and local configuration", third paragraph ("Unknown fields, incomplete setup and unreadable policy get an actionable setup result before provider execution or writes"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", second paragraph ("Before every production write, adopted mode, the installed payload fingerprint/version match and a complete preflight checklist must pass") and binding-order paragraph ("A successor or rollback may activate while an operation remains pending"); Test Plan AC-07 ("performs only separately approved recovery effects under its original policy and current recovery authority"); Tasks T125.
EVIDENCE: The document does not say which consumer policy the original release reads when it runs recovery effects after an upgrade or rollback, and its statements on this conflict. The Design says "Source and authority checks still use current policy". AC-07 says the original release acts "under its original policy". The current code reads policy at the operation's original commit: bootstrap.ts:492 `selectRecoveryRuntime` calls `verifyRuntimeAdoption` with `current_default: options.source_commit` (bootstrap.ts:498) and `collectAdoptionBindings(options.repo, options.source_commit)` (bootstrap.ts:500). Reading the current policy creates two blocks. First, the strict reader rejects unknown fields, so a successor release that adds any setting leaves the original release unable to parse the current file, and recovery always stops. Second, the migration gate requires current adopted mode and a complete current checklist "Before every production write". A successor checklist binds a different component, and a rollback that restores staged legacy routing leaves adopted mode off. That contradicts the Installation rule that recovery effects use the original binding. One implementer reads the original-commit policy, as the code does now. Another parses current settings and hands off every post-upgrade recovery. A third applies the current adopted-mode and checklist gate to recovery writes. The AC-07 control passes all three, because it has no case where the settings schema changes or the mode returns to staged. This traces to the R-90 F-03 correction.
PROPOSAL: State that the original release evaluates the operation's recorded policy at its original source commit for role, publisher and context-digest bindings. Current authority should come from the exact current recovery approval and current native identity and preimage checks, and approved recovery effects should be exempt from the current adopted-mode and checklist gate. Alternatively, state that this case always hands off. Add AC-07 controls for an upgrade that adds a settings field and for a rollback to staged legacy routing, each followed by approved recovery.

KIND: judgement

The operator explicitly selects the original ordinary release for approved recovery effects. A successor or rollback may activate while an operation is pending, but a rollback to a release before version 2 keeps those operations pending. The summary says only that "Interactive execution needs an installed release matching the consumer binding" and that a release update "needs the selected successor checks and a reviewed replacement checklist before production writes resume". It names none of the three. Nothing an implementer builds changes.
PROPOSAL: Add one or two sentences to the summary covering the post-upgrade inspect-and-handoff, operator-selected original-release recovery, and activation while operations are still pending.

Validate those observations against the approved definition through the existing native trial checks"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", unattended-trial paragraph ("Inside the runner, verify the native repository/workflow/actor/event/head against that definition and bind the actual run ID/attempt at launch"); Test Plan AC-07 ("Recover an interrupted native unattended trial against its definition/component/native-launch policy digest before any checklist exists"); Tasks T125.

The definition schema at trial-definition.ts:59 to :60 has no event field and no field for the account that triggers the run. It does have workflow IDs (trial-definition.ts:52 to :55, producer = the trial caller). No existing native trial check compares a run actor or event. It has no actor field and hardcodes `schedule`. authority.md lists "Workflow dispatch" as the trial launch effect. For a dispatched run, GitHub records the dispatching account as the run actor, normally the owner, not the bot. PROPOSAL: State that the launch record's actor is the authenticated publisher's numeric ID and REST login, compared with the definition's `identity`. Name the source of the expected event and head, for example the trigger declared by the committed trial caller and the current default holding the definition. Add an AC-07 or AC-06 control where the owner dispatches the trial and the bot publishes.

### R-93

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans" (line 510: "Execution of exact recovery effects uses the original recorded released component through an explicitly selected ordinary matching install or released-source checkout", "The normal hosted job selects only its committed current release. After a consumer upgrade, that job may inspect the interrupted old operation and then hands off before recovery effects. The operator explicitly selects the original ordinary release for such effects", "There is no automatic receipt-driven checkout or new historical-release configuration list"); same section, "Before each write" paragraph ("Preserve whole-operation original ownership even when the current publisher map changes") and "Publisher selection" paragraph (line 515: "original-operation recovery stays bound to its original account"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", binding-order paragraph (line 525: "The hosted route then inspects and hands off old-version recovery as specified above"); Design "Installation and role execution", third paragraph (line 454: "Rollback selects the previous published checkout/install"); Context "Owner summary", third paragraph (line 79: "After an upgrade, hosted recovery inspects and hands off to an operator-selected original release"); Test Plan AC-05 (line 557, "foreign recovery") and AC-07 (line 559, "an explicitly selected original ordinary release performs only separately approved recovery effects"); Tasks T125 (line 569); plugins/vh-adlc/references/authority.md:230 ("Workflow dispatch").
EVIDENCE: Scheduled operations publish as the bot, and the Design binds recovery to the original account (line 515) "even when the current publisher map changes". The bot credential exists only in the consumer's workflow secret mappings ("A scheduled wrapper supplies its repository policy, native client commands and existing secret mappings"). Interactive entries authenticate as the owner. So for a bot operation, an operator running an interactive install of the original release acts as a foreign account, and AC-05 requires foreign recovery to be refused. The only hosted job runs the current release and must hand off (lines 510 and 525). No committed input lets a workflow run the original release. "Metadata never authorizes downloading or executing a component", and authority.md:230 allows workflow dispatch only under a "trial/cutover grant". The document therefore does not say where the operator runs original-release recovery for the most common hosted case, a bot-owned scheduled operation interrupted before a consumer upgrade, or how the bot credential reaches that run. One implementer adds a manually dispatched recovery workflow with a release input and bot secrets, which needs an authority row the document does not define. Another expects the operator to hold the bot's app key locally. A third treats such recovery as unreachable and sends every case to administrative abandonment, or to a consumer rollback to the original binding. Line 525 leaves that last route unclear too: after a rollback makes the original release current, it does not say whether the hosted job may recover or still hands off. The AC-07 control names neither host nor account, so all three implementations pass. This traces to the R-90 F-03 and R-91 F-02 corrections, which introduced the inspect-and-hand-off and operator-selected original release.
PROPOSAL: State where original-release recovery runs for a bot-owned operation and how the bot credential reaches it. Two examples: a committed manual recovery workflow at the original source commit with its own authority row, or a rollback of the consumer binding so the scheduled job runs the original release. Otherwise, state that such operations always go to administrative abandonment. Add an AC-05 or AC-07 control in which a bot-owned hosted operation is interrupted, the consumer upgrades, and recovery or handoff then proceeds as specified.

### R-94

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans" (line 512: "The entry accepts the exact original operation/source and immutable recovery-selection/approval references and digests, validates them and invokes resume with the selected recovery action", "The authority table must cover this recovery dispatch under exact current approval of workflow ref, component binding, operation/source, selection and recovery scope"); same section, paragraph beginning "Hosted run directories are normally ephemeral" (line 514: "saved as `abandonment-inspection.json` in an owner-private external case directory, and the exact owner approval saved beside it as `approval.txt`"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", trial-grant paragraph ("The unattended synthetic grant is a committed trial definition, selected by path/digest ... Dispatch input alone cannot create it"); Context "Owner summary", fourth paragraph (line 81: "exact approved manual dispatch through the existing hosted entry and secret mappings"); Test Plan AC-07 (line 561); Tasks T125 (line 571), T127 (line 573) and T128 (line 574); plugins/vh-adlc/references/authority.md "Workflow dispatch" row ("Current trial/cutover grant naming the exact workflow and selected inputs").
EVIDENCE: The hosted recovery job has to validate a selection and an owner approval, but the document never says where those live or how a runner reads them. The only concrete approval artifact the design defines is the abandonment `approval.txt`, kept with the saved inspect/restore output in an owner-private external case directory. Restore output and run directories are also private and external. A fresh hosted runner cannot read any of them. The unattended trial grant is fully specified: a committed definition chosen by path and digest, with "Dispatch input alone cannot create it", and the triggering account treated as diagnostic only. The recovery dispatch has no equivalent statement. It does not say whether the approval is a committed record, an uploaded artifact read through the authenticated artifact reader, or just digests the dispatcher types in. It also does not say whether the dispatching account has to be the owner. Careful implementers can therefore disagree. One commits an approval record, which needs its own reviewed commit. Another reads a workflow artifact. A third treats the dispatch inputs as the approval, which makes them self-authorizing; authority.md forbids that for settings, reports and journals. The AC-07 refusals (interactive owner, arbitrary release input, changed recovery scope, unavailable credentials, reuse by the schedule) all pass on the self-authorizing version. This traces to the R-93 F-01 correction, which added the dispatch route.
PROPOSAL: Name the immutable, runner-readable form of the recovery selection and approval, and say what the job verifies and what it cannot prove. One option reuses the committed trial-definition pattern: a path and blob digest that the dispatch inputs only select. Add an AC-07 refusal for an approval that is missing, unreadable or supplied only through dispatch inputs.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "Keep a repository-scoped compare-and-swap lease" (line 502: "host-local, non-shared controller record outside the run directory", "Unknown origin is also observation-only", "A copied run or another host gets observation-only inspection and no automatic lease takeover. Moving execution requires a separately reviewed owner-approved takeover after the original writer and requests are proven settled; its CAS binds the exact observed lease value"); paragraph beginning "Keep durable operation plan/receipt records" (line 510); paragraph beginning "New version-2 sealed plans" (line 512: "Once rollback makes the original release current, that manually selected hosted recovery can run under the original-account/evidence checks"); paragraph beginning "Hosted run directories are normally ephemeral" (line 514); Test Plan AC-05 (line 559: "allow only an exact approved takeover with retained ownership and settlement proof") and AC-07 (line 561); Tasks T125 (line 571); plugins/vh-adlc/references/authority.md delivery acknowledgement paragraph ("An observation-only restored mirror cannot take ownership of a live lease").
EVIDENCE: Hosted run directories are ephemeral (line 514), and normal resume needs the host-local origin record (line 502). That means the manually dispatched recovery job always runs as "another host". It holds only a restored mirror, which "never copies executing ownership" and is observation-only. Line 512 still says this job "invokes resume with the selected recovery action" and "can run". Lease release and acknowledgement both need the owned lease: "releases only the exact owned lease", "retain the lease while another effect remains unresolved", and authority.md says a restored mirror cannot take a live lease. The document does not connect the dispatch route to the takeover rule. It leaves open whether the recovery approval includes the takeover, whether a separate takeover approval is needed, or whether reconcile and retire-receipt may act from the mirror. It also does not say what proves the interrupted hosted writer has settled, for example the original workflow run's native terminal state. One implementer folds takeover into the recovery approval. Another requires a second approval. A third leaves every hosted recovery observation-only, so all of them end in administrative abandonment. The AC-07 bot-owned control does not mention takeover, so all three pass. This traces to the R-93 F-01 correction.
PROPOSAL: State that the dispatched recovery first performs the defined owner-approved takeover. Its approval should bind the observed lease value and the interrupted run's native terminal state. If that is not the intent, list exactly which recovery actions may run from a restored mirror. Add an AC-07 control in which the dispatched job stays observation-only without takeover approval, and takes the lease once by CAS with it.

### R-95

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Context "Owner summary", fourth paragraph ("Retain durable operation records, guarded observation-only restoration and evidence-backed owner acknowledgement"); Context "Simplification basis on 2026-11-15", paragraph declining the suggestion to drop delivery acknowledgement; Context "Terms used here", row "Delivery acknowledgement"; Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "Owner-approved acknowledgement remains available through resume" and paragraph beginning "For a bot-owned hosted operation"; Acceptance Criteria AC-05 ("supports evidence-backed owner acknowledgement").
EVIDENCE: Acknowledgement needs an original-mode journal. `delivery-ack.ts:102` to `:106` returns "acknowledgement-original-owner-required" unless `journal.header.recovery_mode === "original"`. Restoration always creates an observation-only journal (`recovery.ts:271`). The Design says hosted run directories are normally ephemeral, and that "After that runner and origin record are lost, fresh scheduled or dispatched jobs remain observation-only." Acknowledgement also needs "a fresh approval of the current changed resource". `references/authority.md` excludes "standing adopted procedures, unattended controllers" as approval sources for acknowledgement. Taken together, a bot-owned scheduled operation can be acknowledged only while its original unattended job is still running, and the document defines no way for owner approval to reach that job. In practice, acknowledgement covers surviving-origin operations, which are usually interactive ones. Scheduled partial deliveries go to administrative abandonment instead. No existing capability is lost: the current code has the same original-mode requirement. But the summary and the decline paragraph present acknowledgement as a general retained capability, and the owner chose to keep it partly on that basis. The detailed rules agree with each other, so implementers would build the same thing. This follows from the R-94 correction that withdrew the hosted recovery dispatch.
PROPOSAL: In the Owner summary and the acknowledgement paragraph, say that acknowledgement runs from a surviving-origin controller, normally an interactive owner operation. Also say that a bot-owned scheduled operation whose runner is gone goes to administrative abandonment instead.

The categories and the checks each one requires are complete, so nothing an implementer builds changes.
PROPOSAL: Put the upgrade categories in a two-column table (change class, required checks). Alternatively, reword the sentence to "Upgrade checks use the fixed change classes below" so the AC-07 reference to "fixed upgrade rows" still matches.

### R-96

EVIDENCE: The uncorrected entry at dd6039e already meets the proof's literal pass condition. `run-cli.ts:59` lists `retire-receipt` as a supported resume action, so the selector is accepted. With no run directory, `run-cli.ts:135` throws "Create the run with scan, resolve or close and an explicit --source." before `createRunDirectory`. With an existing original run, the call passes `componentForRun` at `run-cli.ts:195` and reaches the final branch at `run-cli.ts:293`, which throws SetupRequired "Receipt retirement remains unavailable in this candidate." FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", second paragraph ("Durable recovery receipts remain retained. The helper exposes no receipt-deletion action."); Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "Hosted run directories are normally ephemeral" ("The authority guide must name this separate action"); Context "Approved follow-up on 2026-11-16", second paragraph ("Source search at `61d5cc0` finds only the `receipt-retire` payload declaration in `manifests.ts`"); Tasks T138 and T127; Test Plan AC-05 row.
EVIDENCE: The correction removes only the resume selector. `manifests.ts:90` still declares `"receipt-retire": ["ref", "expected_object"]`. `parseEffect` accepts it, and `authorizeEffects` grants any parsed effect that has a matching digest grant. A reviewed and approved plan can therefore still carry a receipt-deletion effect that, per the Context, no controller or transport executes. `references/authority.md:228` keeps the row "Receipt retirement | Separate cleanup grant naming exact recorded owned refs/objects after linked proposal/recovery needs finish", and that table also accepts an adopted consumer procedure as the grant source. `references/docs-drift-settings.md:161` says "Receipt retirement has its own exact-ref grant." The administrative abandonment procedure covers only unresolved operations and requires the owner's exact approval. The document does not say what happens to receipt refs of completed operations. `ref()` at `manifests.ts:60` requires `refs/heads/`, so those receipts pile up as branches in the consumer repository with every published operation. T127 implementers could reasonably delete the authority row and the effect kind, keep the row as a manual-retirement grant for completed receipts, or fold it into abandonment. Under the declared proportionality lens, a retained declaration with no caller needs a stated disposition.
PROPOSAL: State that new version-2 plans and grants refuse a `receipt-retire` effect before lease acquisition, while version-1 read-only inspection still parses retained records, and add that refusal to the AC-05 proof. State the completed-receipt policy explicitly, for example retained indefinitely and removable only through the exact-approval outside-helper compare-and-swap procedure, and assign the matching replacement of the authority and settings rows to T127.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", second paragraph (`:588`, "Completed receipts have no automatic expiry. Their separate manual retirement requires the owner's exact approval of repository, refs and observed object IDs, saved inspection/approval evidence, and ordinary Git compare-and-swap removal ... T127 replaces the old automatic-cleanup authority/settings rows with this manual policy"); Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", paragraph beginning "Keep durable operation plan/receipt records" ("Resume or exact cleanup still requires the original sufficient evidence and current scoped authority") and the paragraph continuing "Separately authorized manual cleanup may still be necessary"; Context "Approved follow-up on 2026-11-16", R-96 paragraph (`:121`, "The already separate exact-owner-approved manual CAS policy covers completed retained refs ... This describes the existing explicit cleanup-grant boundary from `references/authority.md:228` ... T127 must replace its obsolete automatic wording"); Context "Owner summary", fourth paragraph (`:81`); Test Plan AC-05 row, last sentence (`:663`); Tasks T127 and T138.
EVIDENCE: The row at `references/authority.md:228` reads "Receipt retirement | Separate cleanup grant naming exact recorded owned refs/objects after linked proposal/recovery needs finish. Preserve foreign, moved or unrecorded refs." The row does not describe an automatic action. It sits in the helper effect table, whose preamble at `authority.md` (chunk 0003, "Each effect below needs its actual current request or an adopted consumer procedure naming that effect and exact scope") also accepts an adopted procedure. The new manual policy keeps none of the row's preconditions. It does not wait for linked proposal or recovery needs to finish. It does not limit removal to recorded owned receipt refs, and it does not preserve foreign or unrecorded refs. So the Context claim that the policy "describes the existing explicit cleanup-grant boundary" is false. An operation counts as completed once its effects apply, while its advisory PR may stay open for resolve, carried delivery or post-merge closure. The old precondition existed for that window. A T127 author following the Context would keep or lightly reword row :228. A T127 author following the Design would replace it with an owner-only rule that has no precondition. The AC-05 sentence "Completed receipts remain retained until a separately exact-owner-approved manual CAS retirement with saved inspection/approval evidence" states a requirement but gives no scenario or pass condition. The abandonment procedure, by contrast, has "Test the documented outside-helper Git abandonment procedure using saved inspect/restore JSON and adjacent exact approval". An implementation whose normal release or T127 cleanup deleted completed receipt refs, or whose guide let an adopted procedure retire them, would pass the AC-05 row as written. This traces to the R-96 F-02 revision.
PROPOSAL: In the Design, require manual retirement only for recorded owned receipt refs of operations whose inspection shows them completed, after linked proposal and recovery needs finish, preserving foreign, moved or unrecorded refs, and drop the "describes the existing boundary" and "automatic wording" phrasing from the Context. In AC-05, add a concrete check: after a completed publish, close and acknowledgement, the receipt refs and objects are unchanged and no transport deletion call occurs, and the documented manual procedure is tested with saved inspection and exact approval, as abandonment is.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Context "Owner summary", fourth paragraph (`:81`, "The helper retains completed receipts and exposes no receipt-deletion action"); Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", second paragraph (`:588`).
EVIDENCE: `manifests.ts:89` declares `"receipt-retain": ["ref"]`, and `parseEffect` validates that ref through `ref()` at `manifests.ts:60`, which requires `refs/heads/`. `trial-definition.ts` (chunk 0003, line 12) treats `publication.receipt_ref_prefix` as a real branch namespace. Under the revised policy, every completed operation leaves permanent branches in the consumer repository, and only a manual owner-approved step removes them. This is a new consequence of replacing the promised retirement. The standard asks the summary to explain "important defaults, permissions, trade-offs" and "material changes since the last accepted scope". The summary states retention but not the indefinite growth of branches, and it does not say that adopted procedures can no longer authorize receipt retirement.
PROPOSAL: Add one sentence to the Owner summary saying that completed receipt refs accumulate as branches in the consumer repository until the owner approves an exact manual compare-and-swap removal, and that standing procedures cannot authorize that removal.

### R-98

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", second paragraph (`shared-docs-drift.md:590`, "Manual retirement applies only to recorded owned receipt refs whose saved inspection shows the operation completed, after linked proposal and recovery needs finish"); Context "Owner summary", fourth paragraph (`:81`, "These refs accumulate as branches until their linked proposal/recovery use ends and the owner approves exact manual CAS retirement"); Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", acknowledgement paragraph and the paragraph at `:620` beginning "Hosted run directories are normally ephemeral"; Test Plan AC-05 row (`:665`), last three sentences; Tasks T127 (`:745`) and T138 (`:742`).
EVIDENCE: The design limits manual retirement to operations whose inspection "shows the operation completed", but the existing inspection outputs have no single completed field. `recovery.ts:45` returns `acknowledged`, `retired`, `invalidated` or `complete` once every effect is terminal. `retained-controller.ts:79` and `:80` report that state from restore and set outcome `collected` only for `complete`. The `resume --action inspect` path in `run-cli.ts` sets `collected` when `pending_effect_ids` is empty, and `inspectJournal` in `journal.ts` counts `acknowledged-delivery` and `retired-absence` as terminal, so `collected` does not separate a completed operation from an acknowledged one. Acknowledged and reconciled operations are terminal and non-successful, and their receipts must be retained (`authority.md`, "Retain its immutable receipt ref"; `docs-drift-review.md`, "the immutable retained receipt ref"). The administrative abandonment at `:620` covers only unresolved operations. So these receipts fit neither path, yet the Owner summary says every retained ref can be retired once its linked use ends. The old row at `authority.md:228` had no completion restriction; the "completed" limit came from the R-97 F-02 revision. "Linked proposal use" can be observed: `report-history.ts:440` to `:450` reads the receipt ref only for an open owned PR carrying the operation marker. The document does not name that condition. The AC-05 rehearsal tests only a positive completed case plus wrong-object and foreign/moved/unrecorded refs. A T127 guide that left out the completion or linked-PR check, or accepted a standing adopted procedure as the approval, would still pass it. Two T127 authors could therefore document different eligibility rules for removing recovery records.
PROPOSAL: State eligibility in terms of fields the existing inspect/restore output already reports, for example restore state `complete` or zero pending effects with no acknowledged, retired or invalidated event, and no open owned PR carrying the receipt marker. Decide whether acknowledged, retired or invalidated terminal operations may use the same manual retirement, and align the Owner summary. Add AC-05 negative rehearsal controls: an operation with pending effects, an open linked PR and approval from only a standing procedure must each leave the ref intact.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Test Plan AC-05 row (`:665`, "Help, usage and public guides list no receipt-retirement action"); Tasks T127 (`:745`, "Public guides and policy rows must describe ... retained completed receipts and exact-owner-approved manual CAS retirement"); Design `:590` ("T127 replaces the obsolete helper-effect authority/settings rows with this manual policy").
EVIDENCE: Read literally, the AC-05 sentence forbids public guides from listing any receipt-retirement action. T127 requires those same guides to describe the manual CAS retirement. `tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/authority.test.ts:31` currently requires an authority row named "Receipt retirement", and `references/docs-drift-settings.md:161` says "Receipt retirement has its own exact-ref grant". The sentence follows the `resume --action retire-receipt` requirement, so the intended meaning is the helper action. A literal text check would still reject a correctly rewritten row or guide.
PROPOSAL: Reword to "Help, usage and public guides list no `retire-receipt` resume action or helper receipt-deletion command, and document only the manual owner-approved CAS retirement."

### R-99

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", acknowledgement paragraph (`shared-docs-drift.md:606`, from "A bot-owned operation whose runner and origin are gone follows the administrative-abandonment procedure" to "Use the same plan, journal and bounded native observations"); the same section's paragraphs at `:618` ("A bot-owned lost-origin case goes to the documented separately approved administrative-abandonment procedure") and `:622` ("Hosted run directories are normally ephemeral"); Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface" (`:592`, "Completed and otherwise terminal operations ... retain their immutable receipt refs indefinitely"); Context "Owner summary", fourth paragraph (`:81`); Test Plan AC-05 row (`:667`); Tasks T137 (`:746`) and T127 (`:747`).
EVIDENCE: The `:606` paragraph says the administrative-abandonment procedure "requires a settled original mutation, direct evidence that all approved fields applied, exact original publisher/resource bindings", tells the operator to "Record it as a terminal decision", to "retain the lease while another effect remains unresolved" and to "Use the same plan, journal". The procedure at `:622` contradicts each of these. There, the procedure is for an "unresolved operation" with "unknown outcomes". It "supplies no delivery or settlement proof", and it removes the exact approved lease/receipt refs by ordinary Git compare-and-swap. Its record is a saved `abandonment-inspection.json` plus `approval.txt`, with "no abandonment helper command or report schema". It also allows lease-only removal while receipts remain. The `:606` wording also cannot be carried out in code. `journal.ts` `validateEvent` rejects an `acknowledged-delivery` event unless `header.recovery_mode` is `original`, and it refuses an unscoped `attempt` in an observation-only journal ("read-only journal cannot dispatch"). A lost-origin restored mirror is observation-only, so no helper can append a terminal decision to "the same journal". Writing one anyway would also break the AC-05 rule "Refuse ... helper-driven effects from a lost-origin mirror". This revision makes the conflict sharper: `:592` now says every terminal operation keeps its receipt refs indefinitely, while `:606` calls abandonment a terminal decision and `:622` removes those refs. The conflict does not come from the R-98 changes. However, context.md says only the accepted `:622` hand-off "with its existing non-success inspection/approval/ref scope" remains. A T127 guide author or T137 tester who follows `:606` would demand settlement and full-application proof before abandonment. That blocks the unknown-outcome hosted case the procedure exists for. Another author following `:622` would not demand it.
PROPOSAL: Rewrite the bot-owned sentences at `:606` to point to the `:622` procedure without restating preconditions. Attach the settlement and full-application requirements, the terminal journal decision and the lease retention explicitly to surviving-origin acknowledgement. Then state that abandoned unresolved refs are the one exception to the indefinite terminal-receipt retention in `:592`.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Tasks T138 (`:744`) and T127 (`:747`); Test Plan AC-05 row (`:667`, "After completed publish, closure and acknowledgement, compare each original receipt ref/object ... Include acknowledged, retired and invalidated terminal outcomes in retained-ref checks").
EVIDENCE: The AC-05 row adds retained-ref comparisons after completed, acknowledged, retired (known-absence reconciliation) and invalidated outcomes, with zero receipt-deletion transport calls. T138's done conditions name only unknown-action parsing, refusal of version-two `receipt-retire` and version-one inspection, plus a general "Preserve receipts". T127 covers guides and policy rows. No unchecked task names the new terminal-outcome retained-ref test as its done condition. The standard asks each task to name "a done condition that is a test or check".
PROPOSAL: Add to T138 (or T137) a done condition that runs the retained-ref comparison across completed publish/closure, acknowledged, retired and invalidated outcomes, with zero receipt-deletion transport calls.

SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", second paragraph (`shared-docs-drift.md:594`, "Completed and otherwise terminal operations, including acknowledged, retired and invalidated ones, retain their immutable receipt refs indefinitely"); Test Plan AC-05 row (`:669`, "Include acknowledged, retired and invalidated terminal outcomes in retained-ref checks"); Context "Approved follow-up on 2026-11-16", R-98 paragraph (`:125`, "including acknowledged, retired and invalidated operations"); Tasks T138 (`:746`, "known-absence retirement").

PROPOSAL: Replace "retired" with "known-absence retired" (or "reconciled as known absent") at `:594`, `:669` and in the R-98 paragraph so the terms match T138 and the `retired-absence` journal event.

No automatic expiry or job cancellation authorizes this cleanup"); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout" (`:714`, "Its owner-approved definition is the scoped standing trial permission ... Dispatch input alone cannot create it. Document this representation in the authority table"); Test Plan AC-05 row (`:738`, "Test the documented outside-helper Git abandonment procedure using saved inspect/restore JSON and adjacent exact approval").
EVIDENCE: This traces to the T141 correction in `correction.patch`. T141 adds two rows to the docs-drift table in `plugins/vh-adlc/references/authority.md` (`:228` and `:233`). It leaves the section rules around that table unchanged. The text introducing the table at `authority.md:200` still says "Each effect below needs its actual current request or an adopted consumer procedure naming that effect and exact scope, except acknowledgement's stronger current-approval requirement". Acknowledgement is still the only exception named. Read literally, the section therefore lets an adopted standing consumer procedure that names "Administrative abandonment" authorize it. That contradicts the new row's "Owner's exact current approval", the Owner summary's "separate exact approval" and the Design's exact observed-object approval. The text after the table at `:245` lists excluded approval sources (standing adopted procedures, unattended controllers, publication/closure permission, general cleanup grants) for acknowledgement only. Nothing in `authority.md` repeats the Design's rule that automatic expiry or job cancellation cannot authorize the cleanup. The same introduction also names only "current request or adopted consumer procedure" as authority sources. It never mentions the committed trial definition that the new `:233` row calls a standing permission. Settings, reports and journals cannot authorize themselves, and the trial definition is selected through settings, so a reader has no stated basis for treating it as a separate source. `tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/authority.test.ts` checks substrings inside each row ("exact current approval", "Dispatch input alone" and so on). It does not check the introduction or the exclusion paragraph, so it passed in `t141-authority-green-host.log` (81 pass, 395 assertions) while this contradiction exists. An agent following the section rule and an agent following the row can reach different decisions about a destructive remote ref deletion. The T141 evidence says the two rows agree with the detailed procedures, but the section's own text says otherwise.
PROPOSAL: Under the accepted baseline, add a task that updates the `authority.md:200` introduction and the `:245` exclusion paragraph. The update should name administrative abandonment as a second current-approval-only exception, extend the standing-procedure, unattended-controller, expiry and cancellation exclusions to it, and name the owner-approved committed trial definition as the only standing source for the synthetic trial scope. Extend `authority.test.ts` to assert those section-level statements, observing it fail first.

### R-106

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery" (the hosted-loss paragraph at `docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md:695`, the bot-owned lost-origin paragraph, and "Before each write, recheck current permission, native repository/publisher identity, source/read-set freshness, target preimage and review/payload validity"); Context "Owner summary", third paragraph (`:81`); Context "Atomic abandonment decision on 2026-11-16" (`:89`); AC-05 (`:728`); Test Plan AC-05 (`:740`); Tasks T144 (`:854`)
EVIDENCE: This revision adds the atomic push. The gap it leaves open was already in the earlier lease-only compare-and-swap. Abandonment is allowed "after lost original ownership". Its only conditions are exact owner approval of refs and object IDs, plus an inspection that "identifies operation/observed refs, unknown outcomes and observed current resources". No condition requires native evidence that the original workflow run or attempt has actually ended. Acknowledgement does have one: `references/docs-drift-review.md` says "The original executor must be quiescent". The per-write recheck list does not include the owned lease, so a publisher still running between effects never sees that its lease and receipt were deleted. Its refs stay unchanged while it runs, so every `--force-with-lease` precondition holds and the atomic push succeeds. A later job, or an interactive owner run that Actions concurrency does not serialize, can then acquire a new lease while the original keeps dispatching. That gives two writers. The AC-05 Test Plan row only covers "A concurrent change to either approved ref". It has no case where the original is still alive with unchanged refs. Two implementers could build different procedures here, and the current proof passes either one.
PROPOSAL: Require the abandonment inspection to record native evidence that the original run attempt has finished, as a precondition and not as authorization. Alternatively, require the publisher to recheck its exact owned lease before each dispatch. Either way, add an AC-05 test where abandonment happens while the original controller is still live.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Context "Owner summary", third paragraph (`docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md:81`, "removes the lease and selected unresolved receipts"); Context "Atomic abandonment decision on 2026-11-16" (`:89`, "all selected unresolved receipts"); Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery" (`:695`, "every selected unresolved receipt"); AC-05 (`:728`); Test Plan AC-05 (`:740`, "every selected unresolved receipt"); Tasks T144 (`:854`) and T128 (`:856`)
EVIDENCE: This traces to the atomic-abandonment revision. The same Design paragraph says "A receipt without a lease therefore supplies no automatic unresolved-versus-terminal classification". The decision subsection says "a receipt alone cannot distinguish an unresolved operation from a retained terminal operation". Even so, the set of receipts to delete is whatever the owner selects, and nothing ties it to the operation that the present lease identifies. The native-data diagnostic shows that the lease's `plan.json` names the operation, from which `receipt_ref_prefix + op` can be read. Without that binding, an approval can include a receipt from an older operation that is actually terminal. That breaks "Completed and other terminal receipts remain retained indefinitely" and the AC-05 retained-ref checks. The document also never says whether an orphan receipt left by an unsupported lease-only deletion can be abandoned later, or must be kept forever. That path is undefined.
PROPOSAL: Define the abandonable receipts as exactly the receipt refs of the operation named by the present lease's plan metadata, always deleted in the same atomic push as that lease. State that a receipt with no lease is never abandoned under this procedure. Add a Test Plan case that refuses a selection containing any other receipt.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery" (`docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md:695`, "A present lease keeps the next schedule pending independently of whether its metadata is readable. An unavailable lease lookup makes collection incomplete."); Design "Resolution, feedback and reports" ("pending publication/recovery remains non-successful"); AC-05 (`:728`, "A present lease keeps a new audit pending"); Test Plan AC-05 (`:740`, "A present lease blocks a green report before model roles and is rechecked after assessment")
EVIDENCE: This traces to the atomic-abandonment revision. The Design limits the lease rule to "the next schedule". AC-05 applies it to any "new audit", so whether interactive `report` runs also read the lease is left open. The Test Plan adds read timing ("before model roles" and again "after assessment") that the Design never states. Neither section says whether the worker and reviewer model roles still run while the lease is present. The native-data diagnostic suggests they can, because they are read-only. That choice changes provider cost and report content on every pending schedule. "Unavailable lease lookup" is also not defined against a 404. `references/authority.md` says "A 404 can mean missing or forbidden", and the diagnostic's proposed helper treats 404 as absent "only after contents access has been established". An implementation that reads an unauthorized 404 as no lease and goes green would still pass "Test foreign/unreadable metadata and a failed lease lookup".
PROPOSAL: In the Design, state which entries read the lease, when they read it, and whether model roles run while it is present. State that a 404 means absence only after repository read access has been established. Align AC-05 and its Test Plan row with that, adding a must-fail case for an unauthorized 404.

Exercise that limitation explicitly without claiming settlement or replay authority."); Tasks T144 (`:854`) and T128 (`:856`, "explicitly demonstrate the unsupported lease-only-deletion limitation")
EVIDENCE: This traces to the atomic-abandonment revision, which moved a disclosed limitation into the criterion and its proof. The pinned `conventions/feature-documents.md` says a criterion states an end state, and that Proof names "a scenario with a pass condition". "can permit" and "may allow" do not set one. A run that stays pending after lease-only deletion and a run that goes green both satisfy the row. So the row also passes an implementation that adds the receipt classifier or new durable record that the decision explicitly rules out ("introduces no completion record"). PROPOSAL: Give the row a definite pass condition. For example: after lease-only deletion, the next schedule reads no receipt, completes fresh assessment, and reports by its own findings with no settlement, replay or abandonment claim. Alternatively, keep the limitation in Design and remove it from the AC-05 criterion text.

T143 also names no concrete failing test, only "applicable native report/receipt tests", while the diagnostic points to `normal-report.test.ts`. T144 includes "Stackweaver scheduled recovery tests". Those live in another repository, so one commit cannot cover the task. They also repeat T128's stale-object refusal and lease-only demonstration. Tasks are outside the normative baseline.
PROPOSAL: Add `run-cli.ts` and the named failing report test to T143. Remove the consumer tests from T144 and leave them in T128.

### R-107

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery": the stopped-executor paragraph (`docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md:707`, "retaining a native execution-status observation linked to that original operation. For hosted execution, record the original repository, workflow run ID/attempt and terminal native execution status"), the case-directory paragraph (`:711`) and the bot-owned lost-origin paragraph (`:701`); Context "Owner summary", fifth paragraph (`:83`, "confirmation that the original executor has stopped"); AC-05 (`:749`); Test Plan AC-05 (`:761`, "saved native stopped-executor evidence linked to the original operation"); Tasks T144 (`:883`) and T128 (`:885`)
EVIDENCE: This gap comes from this revision's answer to R-106 F-01. The Design makes "unavailable operation linkage" ineligible, but it never says which durable record ties a production operation to its hosted workflow run ID and attempt. Only the native trial binds a launch record with run ID and attempt (`:693` onward, `journal.ts:20` and `:87`). For production context, `policy_sha256` "hashes the canonical flat preflight checklist" (`:693`). The lease/receipt commit is `Docs-drift lease ${operation_id} ${header.run_id}` (`journal.ts:517`), and `header.run_id` is `randomUUID()` (`journal.ts:423`), not a GitHub run ID. The tree holds only `plan.json`. The hosted run directory and its origin record are lost by definition in this case. The local case has the same problem: owned-process completion records live in the lost run directory. Two implementers would build different things. One treats every production lost-origin operation as permanently ineligible. That leaves the lease and a pending report on every schedule, with no supported exit for the bot-owned case the procedure exists to handle. The other accepts a run the operator identifies by timing or by an assertion in `approval.txt`. The Test Plan row passes either one, because it never says what makes the saved evidence "linked".
PROPOSAL: Name the durable source that links an operation to its executor, such as the hosted run ID and attempt bound before lease acquisition, the way native trials already bind theirs. Otherwise, state that an operation without such a record stays ineligible and pending with an explicit handoff, and raise the choice as an Open Question if it adds a record field. Add a Test Plan case in which run evidence that does not match the recorded linkage makes the procedure ineligible.

EVIDENCE: R-106 F-02 named this subsection in its WHERE (`:89`). The dated subsection still describes a receipt set the owner selects, and nothing beside it notes the 2026-11-17 narrowing. The Design sections are authoritative and unambiguous, so no implementer would build anything different.
PROPOSAL: Add one dated sentence noting that the 2026-11-17 answer narrows the selection to the present lease and its own operation's single receipt, or reword the subsection to match.
